#5200. Bitcoin Faces a U.S. Jobs Data Week With November 6 Payrolls in Focus

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Bitcoin enters a week with two scheduled U.S. labor-market releases that could reshape investors’ expectations for growth and interest rates. The Bureau of Labor Statistics calendar places September job-openings data on November 3 and October’s employment report on November 6.

For crypto markets, the question is how the numbers change the economic outlook already reflected in trading. A strong or weak headline alone cannot settle that question, particularly when wages, unemployment and revisions tell different stories.

The BLS schedule lists the Job Openings and Labor Turnover Survey for 10 a.m. Eastern on November 3, or 3 p.m. in London. The Employment Situation report follows at 8:30 a.m. Eastern on November 6, or 1:30 p.m. London time.

Job openings offer a view of employers’ demand for workers. Friday’s report provides the broader monthly account of employment and unemployment. These releases cover different reporting periods, so they should be read as related evidence rather than as simultaneous snapshots of the economy.

A market preview can identify the scheduled catalysts without pretending to know the result. Neither a new payroll figure nor a verified consensus estimate is available in the evidence used here. The useful preparation is understanding which parts of the reports could change the interpretation.

The BLS technical explanation distinguishes the establishment survey, which measures payroll employment, hours and earnings, from the household survey used to measure unemployment and participation. One counts jobs; the other classifies people and their relationship to the labor force.

That difference matters when the headline numbers move in apparently conflicting directions. An increase in employment can coexist with a higher unemployment rate if more people enter the labor force and begin looking for work. A payroll gain therefore does not automatically invalidate a softer household reading.

Revisions also affect the trend. A solid new month can look less convincing if earlier gains are revised down. Equally, an unimpressive initial number may sit alongside upward revisions that make the broader picture firmer. Looking across several months helps prevent the newest figure from carrying more weight than it deserves.

One possible response to resilient employment is that investors see less urgency for easier monetary policy. If that interpretation pushes market interest rates higher, it can make holding non-yielding assets less attractive relative to cash or bonds. Bitcoin could face pressure through that channel.

There is another possibility: stronger hiring could support confidence in household income and economic activity, improving willingness to take risk. Weak data can produce the opposite tension, encouraging expectations of lower rates while also raising concern about growth.

These are competing mechanisms, not a fixed trading rule. The outcome depends on the surprise relative to expectations, the rest of the report and how bond, currency and equity markets interpret the same information. A simple assumption that weak jobs always help Bitcoin leaves out half the problem.

A sharp initial price change is evidence that orders have met the news, but it does not by itself reveal a lasting reassessment. The more informative question is whether the move persists as participants digest the supporting tables and revisions.

Bitcoin also has its own flows and market conditions. Crypto-specific developments can reinforce or offset a macroeconomic impulse, making it difficult to attribute every intraday move to a single release.

This week’s calendar gives investors clear times to watch. Its significance will come from the combined evidence on labor demand, wages and employment, and whether that evidence changes the price of money across financial markets.

#5199. Zcash (ZEC) Whale Builds $14 Million Long Ahead of 300% Speed Upgrade

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

An unknown investor has built a position in Zcash (ZEC) worth about $14 million, splitting the capital between the spot market and the decentralized derivatives exchange Hyperliquid. The purchases came amid preparations for the Network Upgrade 7 (NU7) hard fork: on November 5, it is set to speed up block processing threefold.

Judging by on-chain data, the address t1JJnA linked to the investor first bought 3,052 ZEC (about $3.8 million) on the spot market at an average price of $1,250. Then, on Hyperliquid, it opened a 2x leveraged long on 8,000 ZEC worth $10.16 million in two different wallets.

So far, 3,000 ZEC of this position can be verified, with an average entry price of $1,353.83.

In late October, ZEC pulled back to the $1,220–$1,280 range, and the derivatives position went into the red. At its peak, the paper loss exceeded $1.8 million; it now stands at $249,388.

But liquidation will only occur if the price falls to $647.67, which is nearly half of current levels. The investor is clearly betting on long-term growth rather than a quick rebound.

The main argument is the NU7 hard fork, whose final tests the developers completed on the testnet in October. The ZIP-218 update will cut block time from 75 to 25 seconds: the network’s throughput will triple, while the issuance schedule will remain the same.

Along with the hard fork, the Network Sustainability Mechanism (a reserve fee fund for miners) will go live, and the outdated Sprout pool will be shut down in favor of the shielded Orchard architecture.

Throughout 2026, each new Zcash low came in higher than the previous one. An ascending triangle has formed on the chart, and time after time the price runs into resistance at $1,396. Traders on the derivatives market have split into two camps.

Some expect a sharp upside breakout amid inflows into the Grayscale Zcash Trust. Others fear that right after the upgrade activates on November 5, the “buy the rumor, sell the news” principle will kick in.

#5198. Kraken Adds Native USDG Transfers on Arbitrum One

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Kraken has added an Arbitrum One funding route for Global Dollar, giving eligible customers another way to move USDG between the exchange and a blockchain wallet. The October 29 update concerns deposits and withdrawals; USDG trading was already available.

According to Kraken’s announcement, Paxos issues this version of USDG natively on Arbitrum. Transfers on that supported route therefore avoid the need to bridge an Ethereum token before using it on the network.

A trading venue and a blockchain application can both support the same asset while remaining awkward to move money between. Users may otherwise need an intermediate transfer, a conversion or a separate service to reach the network where they intend to transact.

Adding a direct deposit and withdrawal route removes one potential step from that process. A business receiving stablecoins on Arbitrum can evaluate an exchange connection on the same network, while a customer withdrawing can select a destination compatible with the token being sent.

The commercial value is easier access rather than a newly created dollar balance. Moving existing USDG from one place to another does not, by itself, increase the stablecoin’s total supply. Nor does a supported withdrawal route establish that applications have already adopted the asset at meaningful scale.

A bridged token generally adds a mechanism that represents an asset held or accounted for elsewhere. A native issuance route gives users a different set of dependencies to evaluate. The issuer, blockchain, exchange and destination application still perform separate roles.

That distinction is useful because the same ticker can be associated with different technical representations across networks. A token name displayed in a wallet is less informative than the network and contract actually supported by the receiving service.

The direct exchange route should also be understood narrowly. It does not mean every transfer between Arbitrum and Ethereum bypasses the procedures of the bridge a user chooses. Kraken’s claim concerns its supported USDG deposits and withdrawals, rather than a change to all layer-two withdrawal mechanics.

Kraken describes USDG as a Paxos-issued dollar stablecoin redeemable one-to-one, backed by cash and cash equivalents in segregated accounts. That explains the intended monetary relationship, while the Arbitrum integration explains where the token can move.

These are different parts of the assessment. An efficient blockchain transfer cannot answer every question about issuer obligations, redemption eligibility or the terms applied by an intermediary. Equally, a well-understood reserve structure does not guarantee that a particular exchange will accept an unsupported network deposit.

For businesses using stablecoins, that separation helps organize operations. Treasury staff can assess the asset and its redemption arrangements, while payment teams verify the supported route and reconcile the transfer against an invoice or account balance. Both jobs are necessary for a dependable workflow.

The next evidence to watch is whether USDG becomes useful within the applications and trading relationships customers already maintain on Arbitrum. More transfer options can reduce friction, but a network connection alone does not establish deep liquidity or regular payment demand.

Operational details will also affect the experience: the supported token version, exchange crediting process, withdrawal conditions and any geographic restrictions. Those details determine whether a route that looks straightforward in an announcement works for a particular user.

Kraken’s addition is therefore a concrete expansion of stablecoin distribution. Its lasting importance will depend on the activity it enables between exchange balances and onchain accounts, rather than on the existence of another network option in a funding menu.

#5196. DMG Says Client Assets Are Secure and Highlights Layered Transaction Controls

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

DMG Blockchain Solutions said on October 31 that it and its affiliates had not been affected by the Binance security incident and that client assets remained secure. The company described restricted access, separated duties and multiple approval levels intended to prevent one compromised device from authorizing an unauthorized transaction.

The issuer statement brings two distinct questions into view: where a business has exposure, and how it controls the movement of assets.

A company reporting that it was unaffected gives investors information about its assessment of a particular event. That is useful when a disruption at one service prompts questions about losses or interrupted access elsewhere.

The statement should still be read for what it establishes. Being unaffected does not, by itself, disclose every counterparty balance, contractual relationship or potential future dependency. It is possible for a business to have a commercial relationship with a service without suffering a loss from a specific incident.

That distinction avoids turning an asset-safety announcement into a claim of zero exposure that the company did not make. It also helps investors ask more precise follow-up questions about custody arrangements, operating balances and the services needed to keep transactions moving.

The security principle behind divided responsibilities is straightforward: a person or device able to prepare an action need not also have enough authority to complete it. The NIST glossary describes separation of duty as restricting the privileges available to any one user, including through a two-person control.

In a financial workflow, that can mean separating a proposed transfer from the independent checks that authorize it. The purpose is to make an attacker overcome more than one barrier before assets move.

The quality of the separation matters more than the number of approval prompts on a screen. Two approvals offer less independence if both depend on the same compromised credentials, device or source of transaction information. Reviewers need a trustworthy basis for checking the destination and intended amount.

These are general design considerations, not findings about a weakness in DMG’s systems. The public statement describes the company’s approach without publishing the detailed configuration needed for an outside technical assessment.

A description of a control explains what it is intended to prevent. Evidence that the control operates consistently would address a different issue: whether actual access rights, approval records and exception handling match that design.

For a client or institutional counterparty, the appropriate evidence can depend on the service. A review might examine who can change permissions, how unusual transfers are escalated and what happens when an approver is unavailable. The objective is to understand the functioning process, including its less convenient moments.

A public reassurance should therefore sit alongside the continuing work of oversight. It can communicate an incident assessment promptly without substituting for the deeper information that a contractual relationship may require.

Preventing an unauthorized transfer is one part of dependable asset management. A business also needs a way to complete legitimate activity when a device fails, an employee is absent or an external service becomes unavailable.

That creates a practical balance. Recovery arrangements must restore authorized operations without quietly removing the protections that make the system resilient. An emergency path that bypasses every ordinary control would change the security assessment substantially.

DMG’s update supplies a dated company position on asset safety and the control philosophy behind its transaction process. For investors, the useful distinction is between the reported outcome of this incident and the continuing evidence needed to assess custody, approvals and operational continuity over time.

Written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5195. Cardano (ADA) Faces 5747% Liquidation Imbalance as Longs Get Hit in Market Selloff

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Cardano saw a 1,449% liquidation imbalance surge after bulls were caught off guard amid fresh selling pressure.

The crypto market has yet to recover amid dull sentiment following the Binance hack on October 29, which impacted $6.3 billion in crypto assets.

Binance began phased withdrawals on Sunday, processing $4.65 billion of withdrawals against $1.01 billion of deposits, a net outflow of $3.64 billion, the exchange said. It has now covered around 95% of the $6.3 billion stolen, and the FBI formally attributed the theft to North Korea's TraderTraitor group.

Despite this, the crypto market stays in limbo, with most digital assets trading in the red at press time, including Cardano, which is down 1.29% in the last 24 hours to $0.19. The drop, which followed October's decline, caught bullish traders on the wrong side of the market, leading to long liquidations

According to CoinGlass data, Cardano recorded $217,710 in long liquidations while short liquidations came in at $15,020. This scenario, where long liquidations were 14.5 times larger than shorts, yielded an imbalance of 1,449%.

Although Cardano and Midnight ecosystems were not affected by the Binance hack, with no ADA or NIGHT reported to have been stolen, the prices of both were not exempt from the bearish sentiment in the market.

ADA, for instance, is down 8% weekly, unable to surpass $0.197 following a drop to a low of $0.182 on October 30.

According to Intersect's latest update, Node 11.2 is out as a pre-release, bringing Dijkstra closer to ecosystem-wide testing and integration.

The 11.2 pre-release is another important milestone on the way to broader ecosystem validation, bringing the full Dijkstra era hard fork feature set, apart from Leios. It will be used to move DijkstraNet into the Dijkstra era, giving downstream teams a place to integrate and test their tooling.

Meanwhile, Musashi Dojo remains the early testing ground for the Leios-inclusive 11.3 prototype. Work also continues on Plutus V4 cost models, scope documentation, constitutional amendments, and delivery risk management.

In about 3 to 4 weeks (2 at the earliest): Node 11.3 tagged pre-release, including Leios, is expected. SanchoNet is also expected to join the other Cardano testnets in transitioning to the Dijkstra era.

#5193. Binance hackers begin swapping stolen Solana tokens for Bitcoin: Chainalysis

Crypto exchange Gate said it froze two linked accounts as US investigators urged platforms to block transactions tied to the $6.3 billion theft.

The hackers behind Binance’s $6.3 billion theft have begun converting stolen Solana tokens into Bitcoin after largely emptying the wallets used to launder funds on Tron, according to blockchain investigators.

Lookonchain reported Sunday that the hackers had moved 486,970 Solana (SOL) tokens, worth about $46.4 million, and were swapping them for Bitcoin (BTC) through Relay.

The activity involves funds that escaped the freezes imposed after Thursday’s theft, the largest in cryptocurrency history. Industry partners, including Tether and Circle, froze about $3 billion in stolen assets, while investigator SomaXBT estimated the attackers still controlled about $3.5 billion.

Binance said the attack compromised its hot wallets, with early findings pointing to a hacked employee laptop before investigators identified additional breach points. The exchange began restoring withdrawals Sunday, pledged to cover the losses and has been working with US authorities to trace and recover the stolen assets.

Related: OKX draws $445M in net inflows as Binance outflows exceed $7B

Chainalysis said Sunday that most of the hackers’ temporary holding wallets on Tron had been emptied as laundering activity expanded to Solana. It put the value of the Tron transfers at about $500 million.

The $500 million figure counted transfers during laundering, including repeated movements between wallets, SomaXBT said in response. About $242 million actually left the Tron holding wallets, he said.

Chainalysis said Friday that hackers may be using automation to obscure the trail, citing thousands of transactions in a short period. The firm observed funds circulating repeatedly between wallets, sometimes returning to addresses used earlier in the sequence.

Some stolen funds also reached centralized exchange Gate. Lookonchain reported Monday that the hackers had deposited about $250,000 there over the preceding 12 hours.

Gate subsequently said it had frozen two linked accounts and would cooperate with law enforcement. Its statement did not specify how much money was frozen.

The FBI on Saturday attributed Thursday’s theft to North Korea’s TraderTraitor hackers and published wallet addresses linked to the stolen assets.

The agency, US Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation urged THORChain node operators, exchanges and decentralized finance (DeFi) services to block transactions involving those addresses or funds traced back to them.

Investigators had observed the hackers converting stolen assets primarily into Ether (ETH) and expected further laundering through DeFi and instant swap services, the FBI said.

#5192. Tria Lets AI Agents Trade Perpetuals With Restricted Keys

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Tria has introduced a way for AI agents to open and close perpetual futures positions from a user’s account, bringing automated decision-making closer to live trading. Its October 30 announcement says dedicated keys authorize trading without granting withdrawal permission.

The new feature supports Hyperliquid and Decibel. Users can limit how long a key remains active or revoke it. Whether an agent needs approval for a particular trade depends on the connected tool and how it is configured.

Separating trading from withdrawals addresses one important boundary: an agent does not need permission to send the account’s assets to an unrelated wallet merely to place an order. That is a useful distinction in the design of delegated access.

It still leaves the agent with authority capable of changing the owner’s financial position. An incorrectly sized trade, repeated order or unintended exposure can lose money without any withdrawal taking place. Keeping custody of an account and controlling every economic decision made within it are different things.

The practical issue is therefore broader than protecting a key. A trading instruction needs an unambiguous market, direction and size, along with a way to determine whether the intended action actually happened. Natural language can make a command easier to express while leaving room for misunderstanding.

Tria’s API documentation states that both venues trade on mainnet and that there is no testnet target. It also explains that the available environment setting changes where builder or treasury fees are routed, rather than switching trades into a simulated market.

That is an important distinction for anyone assessing an automated workflow. A configuration that looks like a development setting may still authorize an actual financial transaction. Testing an instruction parser or checking that a tool connects should not be confused with proving a live strategy is safe or profitable.

The documentation also describes access to orders, fills, positions and margin information. Those records can help establish what the system did. They are more useful for accountability than a conversational response that simply says an order was placed.

Revoking a key can end an agent’s ability to submit further authorized actions. It does not logically follow that every earlier order has vanished or that every existing position has been closed. Access control and the state of the trading account are separate questions.

A well-designed operating process needs to distinguish between stopping automation, cancelling outstanding orders and closing exposure. Each has a different effect. Confusing them can leave a user believing the account is inactive while market risk remains.

Monitoring also needs to cover the whole sequence from instruction to execution. A tool can receive a request, attempt an order and encounter a rejection or partial completion. The economically relevant result is the position and balance that remain, not merely the initial request.

AI interfaces may reduce the effort required to interact with trading software, particularly when a task involves several data checks or account actions. That convenience is valuable only if the system preserves the user’s intended limits and makes its actions understandable.

Permissions, trade size, approval settings and reviewable records serve different purposes. Restricting one does not automatically supply the others. A trade-only key is therefore one component of a broader control system.

Tria’s launch makes that boundary concrete. Users can delegate supported trading actions without handing an agent withdrawal authority, but they are still delegating decisions with real financial consequences. The measure of success will be dependable execution within clear limits, rather than the ability to turn a prompt into a trade.

#5191. Binance's 2.5 Billion XRP Audit Logs Crypto Reserves Fully Backed Above 100%

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Binance has published its latest Proof of Reserves report as of October 31, 2026. Judging by it, client funds are still fully backed — despite the largest hack in crypto history at the end of the month. Coverage for XRP is 100.99%, and for the Ripple USD (RLUSD) stablecoin, 100.83%.

A comparison of balances as of October 1 and October 31 shows how the exchange's liabilities and reserves changed after the October 29 attack: hackers then withdrew about $6.3 billion from its wallets across ten blockchains.

Immediately after the attack, Binance suspended withdrawals and deposits, so the growth of balances in Ripple assets occurred before the hack. October results:

The main thing in the report is something else: Binance confirmed full coverage of the ten most traded assets, including BTC, ETH, USDT, BNB and SOL — and did so just two days after the hack.

About $3 billion of the stolen funds was quickly frozen directly on the blockchains with the help of partners, including Tether, Circle and Ripple. To cover the losses and return reserves to 1:1, Binance allocated more than $6.34 billion of its own capital. This reserve included the SAFU fund, the Binance Futures insurance fund and retained earnings.

The remaining 5% of losses are in low-liquidity tokens: they are being quietly bought back through over-the-counter (OTC) desks so as not to crash spot prices.

Each client can verify their own balance: the report is built on Merkle trees and zero-knowledge proofs (zk-SNARKs). For the market, this case sets a new bar: an exchange is no longer judged by whether it gets hacked, but by how quickly it stops an attack and whether it has enough of its own money to plug the hole.

#5190. Shibarium's 745% Breakout Reverses: Should SHIB Holders Be Watching?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Shiba Inu layer 2 blockchain Shibarium saw increased activity near the end of October, but it was not sustainable as the trend reversed.

The number of Shibarium transactions jumped to 6,370 on October 31 from 753 a day earlier, representing a 745% surge, according to Shibarium explorer Shibariumscan.

The sharp increase shows a major spike in network activity near the end of October, but it then encountered a pullback as momentum behind the breakout waned. Shibarium transactions have dropped from 6,370 on October 31 to 1,120 on November 1, close to the baseline it has maintained since October.

SHIB holders might want to keep an eye on the reversal, especially since Shibarium continues to be a major factor in the broader Shiba Inu ecosystem.

Shibarium also saw a similar pattern between October 17 and 21, when transaction activity went from 659 on October 17 to 7,300 on October 19. This sharply turned negative to 915 on October 21, 2024.

The 745% breakout and subsequent reversal show why we need to evaluate Shibarium's network growth over the long term. Shibarium's latest readings might give a hint as to whether the recent rally was just a flash in the pan or a more sustainable recovery.

Shibarium remains in 'rebuild' mode as developers carry out quiet upgrades behind the scenes. A notice on the home page of Shibariumscan indicates ongoing indexing of the chain, with 63% of blocks now indexed.

In response to an X user who asked: "when exactly will the Shibarium L2 network & Zama update be completed, when will the bridging of the Shibarium L2 network on exchanges be fully completed, and therefore when will the Bone token listings on all exchanges take place?"

Long-time Shiba Inu community member Mazrael responded: "Gate and Mexc have added some others as well. There is also more than one direct Fiat to Shibarium bone route. Find them in ShibWallet. You want more you ask them. Zama has been postponed as said by Rand. No date shared. Will be visible by all when added. Their gits also tracked on ShibWatch for that."

#5189. ZeroBeta and Lynq Connect Tokenized Treasury Collateral to Lending and Margin

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

ZeroBeta and Lynq are connecting tokenized Treasury holdings to institutional borrowing and trading through an integration announced on October 27. The arrangement allows eligible assets to be pledged as collateral while the underlying holdings continue to accrue interest.

Under the announced workflow, ZeroBeta’s BlueShift manages risk terms and margin monitoring, while Lynq holds collateral and executes pledge or release instructions. The connection uses Lynq’s collateral-lock functionality on its Avalanche-based network.

Tokenization can make an asset easier to represent and transfer in software. For an institution, however, the more demanding question is whether that asset can fit into the processes that finance daily activity.

Collateral is one such process. A lender or trading counterparty needs confidence that an asset has been committed to an obligation and cannot simply be spent elsewhere while that obligation remains outstanding. A visible token balance alone does not supply that assurance.

The integration addresses the relationship between risk calculations and control of the asset. Its significance is less about creating a new investment exposure than about allowing an existing holding to serve a defined financing or margin purpose.

An asset can produce income while remaining encumbered. Interest accrual describes what the underlying holding earns; a collateral lock describes who can use or release the holding under agreed conditions. The two features answer different questions.

That distinction matters for treasury management. A business should not treat an asset pledged against a loan as though the same balance were immediately available to pay an unrelated bill. Income generation does not remove the claim attached to the collateral.

The economic benefit must also be assessed after financing and operating costs. Earning a return on pledged holdings can improve a transaction’s economics, but the borrowing rate, collateral requirements and fees still determine the overall result. The announcement does not establish a universal net return for clients.

A collateral arrangement needs a method for deciding when support is sufficient and when more is required. Asset values, exposure and agreed thresholds can change the amount that must remain committed.

In the announced setup, BlueShift supplies the risk layer and Lynq carries out instructions affecting the collateral. Separating those responsibilities can make the workflow easier to explain, provided participants understand which system calculates an obligation and which controls its execution.

The difficult cases arise when time matters. A valuation change may demand additional collateral while a treasury team is managing other payments. Institutions need to understand how quickly a request becomes effective, what assets are accepted and how exceptions are handled.

Moving those steps onto connected software can reduce manual coordination, but software still needs agreed rules. A rapid message does not resolve a disagreement about valuation, eligibility or the legal rights attached to a pledge.

The next measure of progress is use in actual financing and margin relationships. A completed technical connection creates capability; recurring transactions demonstrate whether counterparties find the arrangement workable.

Useful evidence would include the types of assets accepted, the range of counterparties using them and the reliability of pledge and release processes. Those details reveal whether tokenized holdings are becoming part of routine balance-sheet management.

For the wider digital-asset market, the development illustrates a practical route from holding tokens to using them in institutional finance. The benefit comes from coordinating ownership, control and risk decisions, with continuing interest as one part of the economics. The obligations secured by that collateral remain just as real as the assets supporting them.

Written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5188. Hyperliquid (HYPE) Bulls in Action Amid 703% Liquidation Imbalance

Hyperliquid is witnessing sustained sell pressure across its spot and derivatives markets over the past weeks, although the market is gradually showing signs of a potential relief.

While the market has remained weak since last month, the latest data from Coinglass shows that momentum is gradually being restored, but long traders are still getting wiped out from the Hyperliquid derivatives market.

With the market already showing signs of a potential relief, it appears that bullish leveraged traders are still getting hit by HYPE's current mixed price action more than short traders.

According to the data, a total of $4.49 million in HYPE positions were liquidated over the last 24 hours. Notably, long positions accounted for $3.99 million, which marks 88.9% of the total liquidations seen during the same period.

This means that short traders were barely affected, as further data showed that short liquidations only stood at $496,730, marking just 11.1% of the total liquidations.

As such, the fast-growing crypto asset has witnessed a liquidation imbalance of 703% over the last 24 hours, as long liquidations were substantially higher than short liquidations.

Apparently, this means that traders betting on HYPE's price surge have suffered nearly eight times the losses recorded by bearish traders.

While the intense sell-off seen across the broader crypto market is gradually beginning to ease, Hyperliquid's hourly price chart has suddenly flipped into green territory.

However, HYPE has continued to show mixed price action, hovering around $86 as of the time of writing, following a 2.26% decline in its price over the last 24 hours.

With sentiment already turning positive, it appears that HYPE's long traders may not have timed their bets properly, causing the massive wipeouts of bullish positions.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On Thursday, the 29th of October, TRON [TRX] prices surged from $0.328 to $0.365 in a matter of hours. Attackers who had drained Binance hot wallets for $6.3 billion across ten blockchains rushed to swap their USDT for TRX.

Since Tether can freeze its stablecoin, the urgency to swap to TRX, where no central issuer can block transfers, saw a $234 million USDT swap to TRX on SunSwap, at $0.367 per TRX, blockchain data firm Bitquery confirmed.

At that time, the TRX market price had been only $0.33, which helped explain the swift price surge. Other intelligence platforms have since reported whale wallets with the stolen funds, turning the artificial buying spree into a supply overhang for the global stablecoin settlement giant.

Since Thursday’s price spike, the Layer 1 blockchain’s native token has witnessed a 13.4% price correction. Incidentally, the market structure on the 1-day timeframe had been bearish.

#5185. FATF targets crypto hubs in review of money laundering controls

The watchdog has not named the jurisdictions under review. Initial findings on compliance with its crypto standards are due in June 2027.

Update (Nov. 3, 2026, 7:35 am UTC): This article has been updated to include comments from an FATF spokesperson and additional details from its July 2026 report.

The Financial Action Task Force (FATF) is launching a targeted review of jurisdictions with significant crypto activity to address gaps in how governments apply global anti-money laundering standards.

On Friday, the FATF, an intergovernmental body that sets global standards to combat money laundering, announced that the review would focus primarily on jurisdictions with “materially important” virtual asset service provider (VASP) activity, taking their existing assessment status into account.

A  FATF spokesperson pointed to its July 2026 report, which lists FATF members and other jurisdictions with significant VASP activity. Jurisdictions in the table, including the US, UK, Singapore and the United Arab Emirates account for about 97% of the global virtual asset market.

The spokesperson did not confirm which jurisdictions from the existing list would be selected. Initial findings will be published in June 2027, with further results in October 2027.

For non-member jurisdictions, inclusion in the table requires a more than 0.25% of global crypto trading volume or a top-30 ranking for crypto ownership and adoption.

Inclusion in the table does not indicate a jurisdiction’s illicit finance risk or compliance with FATF standards.

The July report found that 83% of surveyed jurisdictions had adopted crypto Travel Rule legislation, while significant enforcement gaps persisted.

The Travel Rule requires crypto service providers to share information about the sender and recipient of transfers.

The report found that 55 of the 91 jurisdictions with Travel Rule laws had yet to take supervisory or enforcement action over compliance.

Authorities also struggled to identify unlicensed crypto businesses and oversee providers serving local customers from overseas.

Related: FATF urges faster crypto AML enforcement as stablecoin crime increases

#5184. TRON became an early route for stolen Binance funds, Elliptic finds

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

TRON served as a major early route for moving assets stolen in the Binance hack, according to a November 2 investigation by Elliptic. The blockchain analytics firm traced funds spreading across roughly 7,700 TRON addresses before much of the activity shifted elsewhere.

Elliptic’s summary showed how the attackers moved on some of its assets during and after stablecoin issuers such as Tether froze billions of dollars. Elliptic estimated that about $3.24 billion in digital assets were still sitting at the addresses associated with the theft at around 07:00 UTC on November 2.

Binance reported approximately $6.3 billion stolen in the October 29 attack. The FBI subsequently attributed the theft to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group.

According to Elliptic’s investigation, the attackers swapped 234 million stolen USDT for TRX on SunSwap before Tether froze the remaining USDT on TRON.

A distribution wallet began breaking TRX into smaller chunk transfers on October 30. The funds travelled through thousands of different addresses and crossover bridges/services that exchange assets. ​

In total, $147M was seen leaving the TRON network, almost all of which left and reached the Ethereum network as ETH.

A total time of 51 hours was used, and a total of 1,113 transfers occurred from the time the distributing wallet came into service to its last transfer. The last transfer took place on 1 November at 05:33 UTC.

In another November 1 thread, analyst SomaXBT calculated that about $242 million was laundered in roughly two days using TRON-linked routes.

Over two-thirds of the amount was allocated to routes leaving TRON (194M versus 194M). An added $22M was an estimated amount of fees, trading costs, and price change.

In both investigations, the money was moved through several services, ending up in newly created wallets, adding another layer of extra steps investigators had to follow.

By early November 1, TRON activity had largely stopped, Elliptic said. Movement subsequently picked up on Ethereum, Solana, and other networks.

Early on November 2, all 233 million stolen XRP moved to six new addresses. Elliptic also identified about 28 BTC passing through transactions that combine multiple users’ Bitcoin, complicating tracing.

Only a tiny amount was likely cashed out, according to Elliptic, meaning it is still possible for exchanges and brokers to intercept because investigators will be following the assets.

#5183. Solana Officially Scores 1,000 Days Without Outage

The Solana network has hit a significant milestone in its quest to overcome its early hurdles, successfully going 1,000 days without a network-wide outage.

This feat stands in stark contrast to Solana's past, when the blockchain experienced some notable network disruptions. One of the more significant outages occurred in September 2021, when a spike in bot traffic from Grape Protocol IDO transactions led to a surge in transactions that overwhelmed validators and hindered consensus, resulting in a roughly 17-hour network outage.

The Solana network experienced similar problems in April 2022, with a large traffic spike due to an NFT mint that led to a roughly 7-hour outage. Another outage happened in June 2022, when a bug involving durable nonce transactions caused block production to stop for roughly 4.5 hours. Finally, in September 2022, the network experienced a fork due to a bug in the consensus layer, which led to a halt in block production lasting over 6 hours.

The final major outage occurred back in February 2023, when abnormal block forwarding overwhelmed Solana's Turbine block propagation system, leaving validators stuck in a vote-only state and leading to a coordinated restart. This would prove to be a monumental event for Solana in terms of its engineering progress.

Solana has seen a string of updates and engineering innovations by its developers in an effort to improve the network's ability to avoid the failures that had plagued it in the past. Some of these features include stake-weighted quality of service to better prioritize traffic from staked validators. The network also saw networking innovations such as QUIC that provide more flexibility in dealing with spam and congestion. There have also been updates to deduplication logic following the February 2023 outage. Additionally, the network has seen innovations such as a local fee market and other scheduling improvements to discourage users and bots from spamming the network with duplicate transactions.

Recently, Solana has emphasized that network resilience should be taken seriously. In a September 2026 update, the project shared that the network has seen 100% uptime since February 2024 and has continued to handle large amounts of traffic. It also highlighted a major infrastructure provider experiencing a routing failure in August 2026 that caused validators representing nearly 29% of the network's stake to go offline, but this did not result in a halt in block production. The project uses this as an example of increased fault tolerance.

For Solana, this 1,000-day milestone is not just a success in terms of network uptime. It's a statement and a demonstration to the world that a network once mocked for outages can now support serious financial and on-chain use cases.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Less than 48 hours remain before the U.S. midterm elections.

This is the first big election to follow the 2024 presidential elections that sent the crypto market into a frenzy after pro-crypto Donald Trump won.

But now that the odds of a Democratic sweep of the midterms are over 73%, it brings up the question whether crypto will do the same if the Republicans fall short.

The stakes are higher for Ripple. As the chart below shows, XRP had the most explosive rally of the post-election period in 2024. It rose over 280% in November. The key takeaway? November has historically been XRP’s strongest month, with average returns above 80%.

That naturally puts Ripple [XRP] under the spotlight ahead of the upcoming elections.

Interestingly, XRPL is exhibiting a balanced picture on-chain, as the number of transactions is currently hovering around 3.29 million, while the volume of transactions is sitting at just under $2.32 billion. Active addresses are also an important metric to monitor. While transactions are being processed, the adoption of users needs to increase.

In essence, Ripple has been relatively quiet on-chain. This raises a key question: Can XRP’s ongoing supply squeeze provide enough bullish pressure to fuel a breakout, even as on-chain demand remains subdued?

Ripple kicked off November with its scheduled 1 billion XRP escrow unlock.

However, shortly after, 700 million were re-locked, meaning that the net amount being released is actually 300 million. It’s common for a large amount of tokens to be locked back into escrow each month. In the past year, Ripple has unlocked 12 billion XRP but has also locked 8.4 billion.

This means that the real amount of supply coming into the market is far smaller than the 1 billion figure.

As can be seen in the post below, Ripple has placed 700 million XRP back in escrow through three transactions. Simply put, this means that these tokens cannot be released into the market immediately.

After the 1 billion XRP was unlocked, there are now just 300 million XRP available outside of escrow. This means less supply pressure.

Against this backdrop, XRP’s on-chain signals start to matter.

According to SoSoValue, ETFs have so far seen an impressive increase of $4.32 million in weekly inflows, surpassing last week’s $4.25 million, with the best weekly inflows since mid-September.

Combined with recent whale activity, the fact that XRP has been able to stay above $1.20 suggests it could be in an early accumulation phase.

With the midterm elections approaching, this setup puts XRP on the watchlist for a potential post-election rally, provided broader market sentiment turns bullish.

#5181. TRM and Chainalysis Tie $6.4B Binance Hack to North Korea's TraderTraitor

TRM and Chainalysis Tie $6.4B Binance Hack to North Korea's TraderTraitor

Three blockchain analytics firms have attributed this week's hack of Binance to North Korea, with two of the largest naming TraderTraitor, a North Korean hacking group, as responsible for what both describe as the largest crypto theft on record.

TRM Labs said it made the attribution "with high confidence," based on "robust on and off-chain intelligence." It put the theft at about $6.36 billion, moved out of Binance wallets on 10 blockchains, most of it in about 20 minutes from 4:05 p.m. ET on Thursday. That is about four times the $1.5 billion Bybit hack of February 2025, which the FBI attributed to the same group, TRM noted.

Chainalysis, which put the total at about $6.4 billion, said the strongest evidence came when its investigators saw funds stolen from Binance commingling in real time with funds from last month's Bitget exploit, which had been attributed to North Korean actors.

A Chainalysis spokesperson told CNN the firm had "high confidence" North Korea was behind the hack. Singapore-based Pangolin Intelligence also told CNN it believed a North Korean hacking unit was responsible, based on "analyzing the on-chain activity related to this hack and correlating with information gathered from previous known incidents." North Korea has previously denied hacking allegations.

TRM and Chainalysis both said the attacker's operational wallet was funded through Tornado Cash, a crypto mixer. TRM said that and other features, including staging that began well before the theft, matched earlier North Korea-linked attacks such as the Bybit hack and April's KelpDAO exploit.

On Ethereum, the attacker used an EIP-7702 delegation, a feature introduced in Ethereum's Pectra upgrade that lets an ordinary wallet run smart contract code, to turn a Binance hot wallet into a sweeper it controlled, with 50 pre-signed authorizations prepared in advance, according to TRM. Binance wallets on the other nine chains sent funds to the attacker through ordinary signed transfers. In TRM's assessment, that points to a compromise of Binance's hot wallet signing infrastructure.

Binance has said its investigation began with a compromised engineer's laptop, but has since uncovered "additional points of breach." The Bybit attack also began with a single compromised machine: North Korean hackers planted malicious code in the infrastructure of wallet provider Safe after compromising a Safe developer's computer, Decrypt reported at the time.

The attacker has moved quickly to launder what it could not keep. Chainalysis said it is seeing "never-seen-before laundering patterns," with thousands of transactions in a short period suggesting the use of automation to circulate funds repeatedly among addresses. The attacker also sold USDT to market makers and borrowed ETH against USDT on lending protocol Aave to race issuers' freezes, according to TRM, and now holds more than 420,000 ETH and 18,270 BTC, about $2.4 billion beyond any issuer's reach.

Tether, Circle, Ripple and World Liberty Financial have frozen about $2.67 billion in stablecoins, and Arbitrum's Security Council froze 8,426 ETH and 74.6 million ARB, TRM said. Binance puts the total frozen at about $3 billion.

Binance has begun recovering affected assets for users and continues to work with law enforcement, issuers, blockchain intelligence firms and industry partners to trace, freeze and recover additional funds.

The company has already publicly committed to cover losses through… pic.twitter.com/UqtBd37Fpj

In an update on Friday, Binance said it has reserved more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover the incident, $5 billion of which is already held in the exact tokens that were stolen. It is buying the rest through market and over-the-counter channels, which "can take longer because some tokens may have low liquidity," the exchange said. Those figures do not include frozen assets.

"We will weather this storm," Binance said.

North Korean hackers have long targeted the exchange. In August 2025, Binance chief security officer Jimmy Su told Decrypt that North Korean attackers try to get hired at Binance every day, and called them the single biggest threat to crypto companies. On Wednesday, the U.S. Treasury sanctioned 10 individuals and one entity over North Korean IT worker schemes that moved millions of dollars in crypto.

We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Brazilian crypto platform Mercado Bitcoin has confirmed the detection and containment of an issue in one of its systems. As a precaution, the exchange has temporarily suspended deposits and withdrawals, assuring clients of the safety of their assets and the absence of a data leak.

The incident occurred shortly after the platform passed a local licensing deadline.

According to independent blockchain company Hypernative, suspicious outflows from wallets linked to the exchange exceeded $40 million (approximately 217 million reais). Analysts recorded the movement of assets on the Ethereum, Bitcoin, Solana, Tron and Stellar (XLM) networks.

In total, the activity spanned at least 10 blockchains, also including Polygon, Arbitrum, Avalanche and BNB Smart Chain. The first suspicious transfers were recorded at around 02:15 UTC and involved Bitcoin deposit addresses attributed to the exchange.

Among the flagged assets were 106 BTC and 2,852 ETH. The hackers carried out direct token transfers, which indicates a compromise of operational reserves rather than segregated client assets.

Among the withdrawn assets, blockchain analysts recorded a large volume of Stellar tokens: about 6.7 million XLM were consolidated at addresses linked to the suspicious transactions.

As part of the investigation and coordination of efforts with the authorities, Mercado Bitcoin published a specific Stellar destination address to help the community and security firms track the movement of assets in real time.

The move is aimed at monitoring the movement of tokens and preventing their laundering through third-party services.

At the moment, the exchange is conducting an investigation, tracking the assets and promising to provide regular updates. The exchange has not yet confirmed the amount involved, disclosed which system was compromised or given a date for resuming deposits and withdrawals.

Until the platform fully resumes normal operations and the security of its infrastructure is confirmed, users are advised to refrain from making new deposits.

#5179. Cardano Foundation Joins Swiss CMTA. ADA Tests $0.20

Home » Cardano (ADA) » Cardano Foundation Joins Swiss CMTA. ADA Tests $0.20

Cardano Foundation just opened the path for multi-billion tokenized shares on the mainstream Layer-1 network.

The Cardano Foundation has joined Switzerland’s Capital Markets and Technology Association, and the group has recognized Cardano’s CIP-0113 programmable token standard as equivalent to its own CMTAT framework for certifying tokenized shares.

The move advances regulated securities tokenization on the network at the same time ADA trades in a clear bearish structure and tests the $0.182 area.

The Foundation announced membership in CMTA on October 29. CMTA is the Swiss industry association that develops practical standards for issuing and managing securities on distributed ledgers.

Following the mainnet launch of CIP-0113 earlier in the month, CMTA’s Expert Committee treated a reviewed Cardano implementation of the standard as technically equivalent to CMTAT for equity securities.

NEW: Cardano Foundation joins CMTA, the Swiss entity "setting the standard for tokenized capital markets," alongside members including UBS, with $7T in AUM. CMTA has recognized $ADA's CIP-113 as "equivalent to the CMTAT—meaning issuers can use it to tokenize certifiable shares." pic.twitter.com/iqmz811b4U

Issuers that meet the deployment conditions can therefore use it to tokenize shares that are eligible for CMTA’s certification process.

CIP-0113 went live on Cardano’s (ADA) mainnet on October 7 without a hard fork. It lets issuers attach compliance rules.

KYC and AML checks, sanctions screening, freeze and seize powers, and transfer restrictions, directly to native Cardano tokens. The ledger enforces those rules on every transfer, mint, or burn.

The blokchain recognition is limited to tokenized shares (equity), does not extend to tokenized debt or Cardano’s base-layer infrastructure, and still requires each issuance to satisfy Swiss legal and technical checks.

Shortly after launch, Veridian, a digital-identity company spun out from the Foundation, tokenized its own shares as ledger-based securities under Switzerland’s DLT Act using the standard, becoming the first publicly identified issuer to do so.

While the institutional groundwork advances, ADA’s chart tells a different short-term story. On the 4-hour timeframe the token continues a downtrend of lower highs and lower lows. It has slipped below the $0.20 support zone and is testing the $0.182 area.

$ADA / USDT – 4HCardano continues trading in a strong bearish downtrend with lower highs and lower lows 👀📉ADA has slipped below the $0.20 support zone and is now testing $0.182. Buyers need to step in quickly to prevent further downside.🎯 Bullish recovery target: $0.2188… pic.twitter.com/VVA9FFVh1W

A reclaim of $0.20 is widely viewed as the first requirement for any shift in near-term sentiment, with resistance levels clustered around 0.2004–0.2188.

Until that reclaim occurs, bears remain in control and further downside remains possible if buyers fail to defend the current zone.

The CMTA membership and CIP-0113 equivalence give Cardano a clearer route into regulated tokenized capital markets in Switzerland, where ledger-based securities already have a defined legal framework. MSB Intel says the opportunities are in the trillions.

The Cardano Foundation has joined CMTA, the Swiss body that sets tokenized securities standards, whose members include UBS, Pictet and Lombard Odier.UBS alone has $7.328T in invested assets.@Cardano pic.twitter.com/Vb4bVAac0X

No doubt, this is the kind of standard work that makes real-world assets (RWAs) practical rather than purely experimental.

At the same time, the token’s price continues to reflect broader market weakness, with ADA trading near $0.19 and still well below prior cycle levels. For now the institutional plumbing is moving forward even as the chart remains under pressure.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Solana [SOL] could risk further decline based on analysis from crypto analyst Joao Wedson, referencing the asset’s previous midterm election performance.

SOL is currently underperforming, with the asset down approximately 20% in the past 30 days. The midterm analysis, according to the data provided, shows that SOL tends to underperform one year after midterm elections before gradually gaining momentum.

This pattern was observed between 2022 and 2023.

A similar outlook could see SOL drop further from its present level, creating new local lows as investors exit their positions.

While the fractal provides insights into possible market performance, analysis of the liquidation heatmap shows a major possibility of the asset witnessing a significant decline, as there are clusters below the price.

Liquidation clusters below the price often act as magnets that pull prices towards them. Presently, the clusters below the price are of significant depth, extending towards the $73 region on the chart.

The network’s performance remains critical and could be a sting in the tail for investors who may be accumulating the asset.

Artemis reports that there has been a decline in revenue and transaction activity on the network. The total revenue generated by the protocol fell from $1.3 million on October 6 to $442,000 at the time of this analysis.

Declining revenue is an impact of limited on-chain transactions, which Artemis confirmed have also declined across the network.

Transactions on Solana had fallen from roughly 163 million to 120 million at press time. Usually, when on-chain activity declines like this, it often translates to less demand for the underlying asset, which could weaken its price structure and overall outlook.

On-chain, however, there is still some possibility that users in the market have a firm outlook as they anticipate SOL to perform decently. The Total Value Locked (TVL), which tracks deposits across the network, has remained nearly flat.

This suggests that users are neither seeing buying pressure outweighing selling pressure nor vice versa.

In fact, over the past 24 hours, Solana’s TVL rose 1% to $5.416 billion. Usually, a broader bearish outlook would force investors to move locked capital into more stable assets.

If investors are going to pull funds out of SOL heading into the midterm elections, then understanding the positioning of U.S. investors through Exchange-Traded Funds (ETFs) is important.

The spot U.S. SOL ETFs show that there has been a major capital outflow over the last five trading days, with total net flows recording -$71.48 million.

Overall, the total net assets of Solana ETFs remain at $1.4 billion. Given the scale of the recent sell-off, which represents roughly 5% of the total market capitalization, it remains worth noting that U.S. investors may likely sell into the midterm elections.

Solana risks further decline as historical midterm election patterns and liquidation clusters point to a potential drop towards $73.

Network revenue fell to $442,000, while spot SOL ETFs recorded $71.48 million in outflows over five trading days.

“We detected and quickly mitigated a security issue in one of our systems this morning,” Mercado Bitcoin said.

Mercado Bitcoin stressed that the incident did not harm any customers or lead to a data breach.

Nevertheless, as a precautionary measure, the firm decided to temporarily halt deposits and withdrawals while its security teams conduct further investigations into the issue and try to locate any assets that could have been affected.

Additionally, Mercado Bitcoin said that it is working with the appropriate authorities to investigate the incident.

In the latest update, Mercado Bitcoin revealed that it continues to work on restoring its operations to normal.

“We are still working to safely restore operations and will keep our customers updated on our official channels,” the exchange announced.

Interestingly, the company also released some cryptocurrency wallet addresses related to suspicious transactions linked to the incident.

The move aims to assist blockchain investigators and other industry players in keeping track of possibly compromised assets on different networks.

The list includes Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), Solana (SOL), Tron (TRX), Polygon, Arbitrum, Avalanche, BNB Smart Chain and Stellar (XLM).

Mercado Bitcoin is known for having a strong partnership with San Francisco-based blockchain firm Ripple.

#5176. XRP’s ‘Binance Hack 2026’ wallets move 40M tokens as ETF inflows persist

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

XRP’s latest withdrawal surge reflects more than routine whale activity, as Binance’s reported security breach triggered significant movements across exchanges.

At the end of October, there was a sharp increase in big XRP transfers. During that period, roughly 283 million XRP were withdrawn from Binance. Whales’ net outflows reached 226 million.

Investigators concluded that around 233 million XRP were sent to external accounts using compromised Binance accounts. Afterward, Binance suspended withdrawals, resulting in a 94.8% drop in large XRP withdrawals to 14.7 million on the 30th of October.

Withdrawals increased back up to 80 million XRP on the 1st of November after withdrawals started again

If these large XRP are moved off of exchanges, they can potentially decrease the amount of XRP being held on exchanges and therefore limit short-term selling pressure. The fact that the hackers used compromised accounts complicates the indication of accumulation.

Thus, XRP may continue to be volatile due to the uncertain nature of the recent hacks.

The movement of hack-linked funds has added another layer of uncertainty to Binance’s recovery efforts.

In one instance, a wallet labeled “Binance Hack 2026” transferred 40 million XRP worth approximately $50.49 million to another anonymous account also labeled “Binance Hack 2026.”

This implies that instead of simply being left inactive in their respective initial wallets, the hackers’ ill-gotten gains are actively being utilized and transferred on chain. However, the transfer does not confirm liquidation or movement into an exchange.

Instead, it adds more confusion regarding what exactly the hacker will do with the assets next, whether they plan to sell them, consolidate their stash, or set up future transfers.

Therefore, monitoring where the recipient wallet ultimately ends up and follows through on each of its subsequent transactions is crucial.

Ultimately, any movement toward exchange-linked addresses could increase concerns about potential selling pressure and recovery efforts.

Despite growing fears that the hack-related movements may result in a decrease in XRP’s exchange liquidity, institutional flows tell a more resilient story. Weekly inflows into XRP spot ETFs totaled $4.32 million by the end of October.

These inflows came after a one-week withdrawal of $1.84 million from those same ETFs occurred on the 16 of October. It appears that investors continued adding capital to these investment products regardless of ongoing market issues and uncertainty surrounding XRP.

Prior to this period of inflow, the funds investing in these ETFs attracted $11.31 million during the week ending on the 9th of October. As such, cumulative net inflows into these investment vehicles reached $1.81 billion.

However, total net assets declined from $1.66 billion to $1.42 billion throughout October as XRP’s market value weakened. This contrast suggests fresh institutional buying has not fully offset valuation losses, leaving ETF demand supportive but insufficient to reverse broader price pressure.

Ripple saw hack-linked Binance outflows surge, raising concerns over stolen funds and potential selling pressure.

XRP ETFs sustained inflows, but falling net assets highlight weak price performance.

#5175. Morning Minute: Binance’s $6.4B Hack Overshadows Hyperliquid’s Biggest Day

Morning Minute: Binance’s $6.4B Hack Overshadows Hyperliquid’s Biggest Day

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

Someone stole about $6.4 billion from Binance. That is, by a wide margin, the biggest crypto theft that has ever happened, four times the $1.5 billion taken from Bybit in February 2025.

The money left across three different blockchains: roughly $3.5 billion in ETH, $1.4 billion in BTC, and $630 million on Tron (final accounting still underway).

Luckily, stablecoin issuers can freeze their own tokens; it’s a kill switch built into USDT and USDC. Tether and Circle used it, locking up about $2.39 billion between them. That’s 43% of the haul. That leaves somewhere between $2.6 and $3.2 billion still out there.

Aave also stepped in, freezing its V3 and V4 markets on Ethereum. That’s precautionary, given the worry is an attacker using stolen money to borrow against and leave the protocol holding bad debt.

As this was playing out, Binance stated it’s “aware of reports of a potential security issue” and that user funds are “completely safe,” and paused withdrawals on multiple networks. The clearest comment came from CZ, who no longer runs Binance but is its largest shareholder: it “does look like a hack,” and he expects user funds to be “fully covered.” Later in the evening, Binance announced that it has reserved over $6.34B in company funds to cover losses. So funds are indeed “safu.”

ZachXBT shared that the Binance hack funds were commingled with funds from the $388M Bitget exploit, leading him to believe it’s the same hacker group out of North Korea behind the attack. As for how it happened, Binance pointed to a compromised DevOps engineer’s laptop which was compromised, allowing the attacks to submit the unauthorized transactions, before identifying "additional points of breach" in the following hours.

Markets did what you’d expect. Bitcoin fell below $797,000, ETH fell sub-$2,200 and BNB sub-$700. Over $1.33B in positions got liquidated on the day.

Unfortunately, this came after what was shaping up to be one of the most positive days for a crypto project in its history.

Earlier on Thursday, Trade.xyz announced that Intercontinental Exchange (the company that owns the New York Stock Exchange) is investing in it. The plan is a regulated U.S. venue offering round-the-clock perpetual futures built on Hyperliquid: stocks, indices, commodities, currencies and crypto, with its own exchange and clearing setup, pending CFTC approval.

Stanley Druckenmiller is joining as well, the first of a small group of outside market figures. He already owns the trade; his family office disclosed a $23 million position in Hyperliquid Strategies earlier this year.

So Hyperliquid has its path to a fully regulated perps offering inside the U.S., claims Stanley Druckenmiller as an investor (and a tout on X), and ICE’s CEO said that Hyperliquid was “bigger than NASDAQ.” Doesn’t get much better than that.

I am excited to be an investor in trade(xyz) as they partner with ICE to bring 24/7 perpetual futures on Hyperliquid to the United States. As a market participant, I am looking forward to to using their innovative product to enhance my investment process.

— Stan Druckenmiller (@standuquesne) October 29, 2026

HYPE shot as high as $97 on the day, up more than 20% at peak, before falling to $89 in the Binance hack aftermath. As of this morning, it’s at $93 and still up 8% on the day.

Unfortunately, now we have to deal with the Binance hack aftermath. Hyperliquid is a likely winner as a primary DEX option vs CEXs like Binance (which will likely be less trusted now.) But there’s also a good chance of the entire market being dragged down by fears of more exploits looming.

We will find out soon enough. Expect a bumpy ride…

#5174. Sui’s 40M TPS Demo Comes With an Off-Chain Caveat

Home » Trendsetter News » Sui’s 40M TPS Demo Comes With an Off-Chain Caveat

Sui’s 40M TPS demonstration highlights blockchain’s push for speed, but its off-chain design raises questions about the actual figures.

Sui reached more than 40 million transactions per second in a demonstration highlighted by crypto commentator Linda, but those transactions did not run directly through its mainnet.

The demonstration, which she says took place at Sui Base Camp during Token2049 in early October, used “tunnels” to process activity off-chain before settling results on-chain. That makes it a different proposition from sustained mainnet throughput.

Linda connects the test to Sui chain push into AI-agent infrastructure. A Sui chain co-founder’s clip featured in the video describes plans for agent coordination, payments and auditable activity, alongside collaboration with Google.

The strategic ambition is clear; the demonstration alone does not establish production demand.

She describes potential private versions of Ethereum applications such as Uniswap and Aave, plus private swaps accessible through existing Solana wallets.

She also reports that Midnight has passed one million mainnet transactions and that daily transactions increased nearly 400-fold in a month.

The YouTube video does not supply the starting daily transaction count, limiting what that growth multiple says about sustained adoption.

Linda reports that Solana’s Alpenglow upgrade is live on mainnet beta, citing a reduction in finality from roughly 12.8 seconds to 150 milliseconds. Those figures imply about an 85-fold improvement, rather than the approximately 40-fold increase stated in the narration.

For Cardano, she describes Leios as approaching rollout through the Dijkstra hard fork, with initial throughput expected at three to five times current levels and later improvements targeting 10–15 times. These remain forward-looking claims in the video, not demonstrated performance results.

#5173. NEAR Intents Head Names 3 Survival Rules for Crypto Wallets

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The end of October 2026 was marked by the largest incident in the history of the crypto market — the $6.3 billion hack of the Binance exchange. This case exposed the vulnerability of the instant exchange (swaps) infrastructure.

While the automated systems of the North Korean group TraderTraitor withdrew $242 million via the Tron blockchain in the first two days, decentralized bridges and liquidity aggregators de facto became the main tool for legalizing the stolen funds.

Against this backdrop, the head of NEAR Intents Alex Shevchenko issued a warning to cryptocurrency wallet developers. His position is pragmatic: focusing exclusively on finding the best rate for the user turns services into unwitting accomplices in cybercrimes.

Since around $3.5 billion still remains under the hackers’ control after a wave of emergency freezes, regulatory pressure on the Web3 sector will inevitably increase. To minimize risks, the head of NEAR Intents formulated three basic survival rules for modern crypto wallets when choosing integration partners:

For wallet developers, the problem is structural. A single swap often passes through several protocols at once — an aggregator, a bridge and multiple liquidity pools — and each hop dilutes responsibility.

If any link in this chain processes tainted funds, the wallet through which the transaction was initiated shares the reputational damage and, increasingly, the legal exposure.

This is why due diligence on integration partners is shifting from an optional step to a core product requirement.

Investments in compliance and integration security are no longer a boring formality. The market has reached a point where the reputational and legal cleanliness of a service is valued far higher than saving a few cents on fees.

#5172. 984M BEAM stolen, then burned – The move that turned a hack into a pump

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

BEAM is retracing after days of a strong upswing. After Binance was hacked three days ago, the attacker stole 984 million BEAM.

In response, the team made a completely unexpected move, burning all the stolen funds while they sat in the attacker’s wallet. As such, the attacker never got to sell these tokens.

This token burn reduced supply, increased scarcity, and resulted in a price pump. As a result, BEAM climbed to a high of $0.0063. Since then, however, this momentum has faded, and the altcoin has retraced.

In doing so, BEAM breached $0.006 and fell below $0.005, touching a low of $0.0048. As of this writing, BEAM was trading around $0.0049, down 15% on the daily charts.

BEAM’s rally was mainly driven by supply shock. Historically, such upside moves have been short-lived, and the current rally was no different.

Investors who chased this rally have since turned bearish and begun exiting their positions. On the Spot side, sell volume exceeded buy volume for the first time in four days, rising to 508 million while sell volume fell to 459 million.

As a result, the Spot Buy/Sell delta turned negative, falling to -48 million, a clear sign of aggressive Spot selling.

On the derivatives side, investors were even more bearish. According to CoinGlass data, Open Interest plunged 25% to $14.4 million, while the derivatives volume fell 22% to $75 million.

Falling OI alongside the volume suggested reduced market participation while traders closed their positions. This suggested a shift in sentiment toward risk-off.

Often, when sellers dominate both the Spot and derivative markets, market conditions weaken, leading to a price decline.

BEAM plunged as speculative demand, driven by the recent token burn, faded. As a result, the upside momentum has weakened while the downside pressure has strengthened.

A look at the Relative Strength Index RSI confirms this view. The RSI fell from overbought to 68. The falling RSI suggests weakening buying pressure as sellers retake the market.

However, they are yet to retake it fully. If the selling pressure continues, RSI will drop towards 50, confirming this bearish takeover.

In doing so, BEAM will likely drop to $0.0033. To invalidate this bearish outlook, the altcoin needs to reclaim $0.005 and see a close above $0.0057.
