#5171. Binance taps half its SAFU Bitcoin holdings, releases reserve proof

Binance’s SAFU Bitcoin wallet now holds about 7,500 BTC after a 7,499 BTC transfer linked to the hack, with no timeline for restoring the $1 billion fund.

Binance’s publicly disclosed emergency Bitcoin wallet held roughly half its previous balance on Monday after the exchange tapped its user protection fund to cover losses from Thursday’s $6.3 billion hack.

The exchange confirmed a transfer of 7,499 Bitcoin (BTC) on Saturday, linking it to the partial deployment of its Secure Asset Fund for Users (SAFU).

As of Monday, the wallet held about 7,500 BTC, worth $583 million, down from about 15,000 BTC before the transfer.

The theft, the largest in cryptocurrency history, forced Binance to suspend withdrawals while it worked to replace the stolen assets. The FBI attributed the attack to North Korea on Saturday, identifying the hackers as TraderTraitor.

Binance began reopening withdrawals in phases on Sunday and said it had replenished approximately 95% of the affected assets, up from 90% a day earlier. It continued buying the remaining, less-liquid tokens through market and over-the-counter transactions.

The exchange is using its corporate treasury, SAFU and part of its futures insurance fund to cover the losses.

Binance’s SAFU policy outlines regular monitoring and rebalancing of the fund’s market value.

“If the fund’s market value falls below US $800 million due to BTC price fluctuations, Binance will rebalance the fund to restore its value to $1 billion,” it states.

Related: Binance hackers begin swapping stolen Solana tokens for Bitcoin: Chainalysis

The policy does not specify a timeline for replenishing funds withdrawn following a security incident.

Binance did not immediately respond to Cointelegraph’s questions about SAFU’s remaining reserves, further deployments and plans to replenish the fund.

Binance said Monday that users could now verify their balances against its Saturday proof-of-reserves snapshot after it completed the accompanying cryptographic proof.

The exchange said the snapshot showed customer holdings in 10 cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin, Ether and USDT, were backed at least 1:1.

The exchange plans to take another snapshot covering more tokens on Wednesday, with the accompanying proof expected to take another 40 to 50 hours to generate.

#5170. HashPack Adds HBAR Tokenized Stock Without LP Reliance

Home » Trendsetter News » HashPack Adds HBAR Tokenized Stock Without LP Reliance

HashPack users can now access tokenized stocks and ETFs through SODAX using HBAR or Hedera’s wrapped USDC version.

HashPack users can now buy tokenized stocks and ETFs through SODAX without relying on liquidity pools on Hedera, according to CEO and co-founder May. Speaking with the host about the wallet’s latest upgrades, she described an integration that lets users pay with HBAR or Hedera USDC while accessing liquidity outside the network.

That distinction matters for larger trades. Assets traded through shallow pools can suffer significant price slippage; May said the SODAX integration avoids depending on locally supplied liquidity to execute purchases.

According to May, buyers receive an HTS token representing a Robinhood stock token issued on Robinhood Chain. She said SODAX maintains the one-to-one relationship, giving users exposure to the underlying asset’s price without requiring another wallet or a manual move to another blockchain.

“You’re not relying on liquidity that exists on the network,” she said. Previously, most assets available to trade through HashPack depended on SaucerSwap pools, apart from token-launch platforms that subsequently created pools there.

May also described dividends as being reinvested into the same stock rather than distributed as cash, qualifying that explanation with “as far as I know.”

The interview did not establish the products’ legal ownership rights, redemption terms or geographic restrictions; details investors would need before treating them as interchangeable with brokerage-held shares.

She sees potential for lending and borrowing against tokenized stocks, although the discussion did not establish that those functions are available inside HashPack.

The stock integration accompanies HashPack’s “Portfolio” redesign, which adds a customizable homepage and bottom-opening navigation dock. May said most users access the wallet on mobile, and its growing collection of swaps, staking options and NFT integrations had made features harder to find.

Enterprise integrations remain a slower prospect. HashPack has ongoing discussions, May said, but nothing particularly exciting to flag for the short term; she suggested developments could emerge over 12 to 24 months.

#5168. Lisk Officially Shuts Down Blockchain After 10 Years

Long-standing blockchain project Lisk has officially ended its blockchain operations after 10 years of existence, moving on to a new financial platform for businesses.

Lisk announced on Nov. 2 the official shutdown of its blockchain after 10 years of existence. This marks the end of one of the longest-lived blockchains in the crypto world.

"Today, the Lisk Chain has officially shut down," the project said in an X post.

With the blockchain shutdown, Lisk has ended its Portal and Bridge services. The rest of the blockchain's infrastructure is also being powered down.

The blockchain shutdown marks a significant milestone in the evolution of the Lisk project, which was launched in 2016 and has seen several changes over the years.

The Lisk team, however, has noted that the shutdown of the blockchain does not spell the end of the Lisk project.

The company is transforming into a new financial platform, one that enables businesses to manage both traditional currencies and stablecoins in one place.

"For 10 years, Lisk built onchain with developers, stakers and holders that made Lisk what it is," Lisk said.

The Lisk project has made it clear that its native token, LSK, will continue to exist even as the blockchain closes shop.

According to the team, LSK will still exist on Ethereum as the loyalty token for its new business financial platform.

In addition, the project announced plans to burn approximately 5.23 million LSK tokens left in its staking rewards pool.

The total figure includes unallocated rewards to users as well as unclaimed earned rewards.

The LSK tokens are set to be burned, permanently removing them from circulation. This will also lower the overall supply of the cryptocurrency.

This comes after a large burn of LSK tokens in September. At the time, Lisk burned 100 million LSK tokens after making the decision to wind down its decentralized autonomous organization (DAO) through a governance vote.

The burn event reduced the LSK token's supply from 400 million to 300 million LSK.

Lisk intends to introduce new use cases for LSK in 2027. This will allow businesses to earn rewards in the token and use LSK for platform fees.

#5167. Israel Extradites Alleged Nomad Bridge Exploiter to US Over $2.8M Theft: Report

Israel Extradites Alleged Nomad Bridge Exploiter to US Over $2.8M Theft: Report

Israel has extradited an Israeli-Russian citizen to the United States to face charges over the alleged theft of about $2.8 million from crypto bridge Nomad, Israel's State Attorney's Office announced on Thursday, according to a report in The Jerusalem Post.

Alexander Block, formerly known as Alexander Gurevich, was transferred to U.S. authorities early Thursday after unsuccessful appeals in Israel. He was arrested at Ben Gurion Airport in May 2025 while preparing to fly to Russia, shortly after changing his surname and obtaining a passport in his new name.

A federal grand jury in the Northern District of California indicted Block in August 2023 on four counts of computer fraud, one count of transporting stolen property and three counts of money laundering, according to Israel's Supreme Court judgment in the case. The U.S. requested his extradition in December 2024.

According to the U.S. extradition request, as set out in the judgment, a June 2022 software update left a flaw in Nomad's smart contracts that let users receive tokens on one blockchain without locking up the equivalent assets on another. Block, operating under the username "Bitlik," allegedly made six test transfers before carrying out four main transfers around August 1, 2022, receiving tokens worth more than $2.8 million. He then allegedly moved the tokens through several wallets and converted them into other cryptocurrencies to conceal their origin.

His actions exposed the flaw to other Nomad users, who drained a Nomad-controlled wallet holding more than $186 million in user funds, the request alleges. A few days later, Block returned some of the tokens and contacted a senior Nomad official on Telegram, offering to return the rest in exchange for payment for finding the flaw. He later cut off contact, and Nomad went to U.S. law enforcement.

The Jerusalem District Court approved Block's extradition in July 2025. On appeal, he argued that the transactions used functions available within Nomad's automated system, and that the public record of blockchain transfers undermined the money laundering allegations.

The Supreme Court rejected both arguments, holding that exploiting a programming flaw could constitute unlawful computer intrusion even where the software technically permitted the action, The Jerusalem Post reported. It also found that publicly visible transactions do not necessarily identify the people behind wallets or rule out efforts to conceal where funds came from.

Supreme Court President Isaac Amit later rejected Block's request for a further hearing, according to the State Attorney's Office.

The August 2022 exploit drained Nomad of nearly all its funds. Because it took little technical knowledge to copy the original attack, Paradigm researcher Sam Sun likened what followed to "a frenzied free-for-all," Decrypt reported at the time.

In December 2025, the Federal Trade Commission proposed a settlement with Illusory Systems, Nomad's operator, saying the bridge lost $186 million after hackers exploited a poorly tested software update. The settlement would require the company to return recovered funds to users who have not been reimbursed. Nomad recovered about $22 million in the days after the hack.

#5166. 5 Best AI Crypto Trading Bots

Home » Cryptopedia » 5 Best AI Crypto Trading Bots

Compare five AI crypto trading bots by automation features, strategy tools, supported exchanges, ease of use, and key limitations.

AI crypto trading bots help traders automate strategies, analyze market data, and manage trade execution without monitoring charts around the clock. However, not every crypto trading bot marketed as AI-powered uses artificial intelligence to make trading decisions. Some platforms rely primarily on predefined rules, while others use AI to build strategies, interpret market conditions, or configure automated trading systems.

The best AI crypto trading bot depends on what you need: strategy automation, AI-assisted decision-making, beginner-friendly controls, or trading across multiple exchanges. This guide compares five platforms based on their available features, intended use cases, and practical limitations.

We looked at each platform’s automation features, strategy tools, backtesting options, supported exchanges, ease of use, pricing, and risk-management features. We also checked the information against each platform’s official website. Our picks are based on the features each bot offers and who it may suit best, rather than independently tested trading results. Keep in mind that features, fees, and exchange support can change over time.

3Commas is a crypto trading platform that combines automated bots with tools for managing and executing trades. It is widely used for multi-exchange order execution and parameter-driven  3Commas AI Trading Bot setups.

Its features include Dollar-Cost Averaging (DCA) bots, grid bots, signal-based trading, and SmartTrade order management.

The platform also offers an AI trading assistant that lets users describe a strategy in natural language. According to 3Commas, the assistant can configure a bot, run a backtest using historical market data, and prepare the strategy for launch.

Limitation: Bot capacity on the entry plan is small, and higher limits require Pro or Expert. Backtests also cannot fully reproduce future market conditions, slippage, liquidity constraints, or unexpected volatility.

3Commas is worth considering for traders who want to automate established trading strategies while retaining control over entry conditions, position sizing, and exits. It is particularly relevant to users who already understand concepts such as DCA, grid trading, and technical indicators.

Cryptohopper is a cloud-based engine that combines automated crypto trading with strategy-building, backtesting, and AI-assisted tools.

Its AI Strategy Designer helps traders choose strategies, signal providers, and TradingView alerts based on current market conditions. However, access depends on the subscription plan, so it’s worth checking the requirements before signing up.

Cryptohopper also has Cryptohopper.AI, a separate tool that lets users create trading bots and other trading tools simply by describing what they want in plain language. Although both use AI, they serve different purposes and shouldn’t be confused with each other.

Limitation: Access to certain AI features depends on the product and subscription tier. AI-generated strategies still require testing and monitoring before live deployment.

Cryptohopper trading bot suits traders who want to experiment with different strategies and automate their selection or execution. It is also relevant to users interested in building customized crypto trading tools without writing all the code themselves.

Pionex is a cryptocurrency exchange with AI trading bots built directly into its platform. Unlike third-party automation services that require users to connect exchange accounts, Pionex integrates bot functionality into the exchange itself.

Pionex offers 16 bots, including Grid Trading, Infinity Grid, Trailing Buy/Sell, Rebalancing, and Spot-Futures Arbitrage bots. The options also include PionexGPT, an AI-related feature that allows users to write custom trading strategies using natural language prompts.

Limitations:  Funds are held on the Pionex exchange rather than on an exchange you already use, so the AI trading bots cannot be applied to other exchanges.

Pionex is a good option for beginners who want to try automated trading without paying for a separate bot service or connecting to another exchange. It can also be useful for experienced traders who prefer to manage everything on one platform.

Coinrule focuses on rule-based crypto trading automation. Users can create strategies by setting simple rules rather than building a trading system entirely from scratch.

With features like Coinrule AI Cloud, users can also use plain language to create trading strategies, get help setting them up, test them against historical data, and connect with compatible AI assistants.

Limitation: Rule-based automation follows its configured conditions; it does not inherently understand market context or predict future prices. It doesn’t provide the level of mathematical detail and precise order book analysis that professional institutional traders need.

Coinrule is suitable for traders who know what conditions they want to trade but do not want to write the underlying code. It is also useful for users who want to test systematic strategies before connecting them to live trading.

Bitsgap is a crypto trading platform that brings everything together in one place, including automated crypto AI trading bots, portfolio management, analytics, and exchange connections. It offers different trading bots, such as Grid and DCA, to help users automate their strategies. Its AI Assistant also helps users configure their trading setups.

With support for multiple crypto exchanges, Bitsgap lets users manage their trading activities through a single interface.

Limitation: Futures bots are not available on the Basic plan.Exchange support and available features vary.

Bitsgap is a good option for traders who use multiple exchanges and want to manage their trading bots in one place. Its automation tools and analytics also make it easier to track strategies and monitor performance.

The best AI crypto trading bot is the one whose capabilities match your trading approach, not necessarily the one with the most AI features.

Before choosing a platform, compare these factors:

AI crypto trading bots can automate execution and reduce the need for constant manual monitoring, but profitability is never guaranteed. Results depend on strategy design, market conditions, execution costs, liquidity, and risk management.

Historical backtests are useful for evaluating a strategy, but they do not establish that it will perform similarly in live markets. Traders should test bots in simulation where possible, start with limited exposure, and monitor performance after deployment.

An AI label alone is not evidence of a trading advantage. Before selecting a bot, establish what the AI actually does, how trades are triggered, and how losses are managed.

The answer depends on the use case. Pionex requires no API setup or subscription, 3Commas builds bots from plain-English prompts, Bitsgap covers 17+ exchanges, and Cryptohopper offers a strategy marketplace.

No profitability can be assumed. Results depend on settings, fees, and market conditions, and losses are possible.

It depends on the platform. 3Commas states it is non-custodial and connects through APIs, while Pionex operates as an exchange where funds are deposited.

#5165. Pando Rings hacker moves 4,000 ETH through Tornado Cash after 4 years – Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Four years after the 2022 Pando Rings exploit, the attacker is once again using Tornado Cash to launder the stolen funds. The wallet first converted approximately $16.98 million in Dai [DAI] to 7,548 Ethereum [ETH] and began dividing the assets into multiple small transfers.

Multiple transactions were found showing a series of 100 ETH deposits, each worth approximately $229,000.

To move their funds, the attacker initially used Aave [AAVE] to convert stablecoin holdings to ETH before sending them through Tornado Cash. At the end of October, the attacker supplied 16.8 million USDT as collateral to borrow 5,000 ETH and repay the loan.

After repaying the loan, the attacker withdrew the collateral and exchanged 16.7 million DAI for 7,548 ETH. These steps show how the attacker consolidated funds by lending and swapping tokens before laundering them.

Thereafter, the attacker made a partial deposit of 4,000 ETH to Tornado Cash and left 3,548 ETH outside the mix. In doing so, this transfer complicated both the recovery of the funds and obscured the eventual destination of the funds.

However, it did not establish whether the funds were immediately sold or if there was widespread selling pressure on exchanges.

As the exploiter continues to break up and hide the trail of these funds, he also increases the chances of distributing additional funds and complicating recovery efforts. Still, the deposits themselves do not establish that the exploiter has either sold his ETH or transferred it to exchanges.

Tornado Cash complicates the recovery process. This is because it removes the link between a user and other parties in a transaction. For example, when multiple users place their funds into Tornado Cash, the funds enter a shared pool.

Once placed in the pool, funds may be returned to users through a new wallet address. As such, investigators can rely less heavily on tracking wallets directly related to the theft.

This creates another challenge for investigators, who must rely on transaction patterns and off-chain intelligence to identify where the ETH eventually moves. The exploiter’s earlier 800 ETH deposit in August also shows that this approach predates the latest transfers.

However, the current deposits have yet to generate any connections to wallets associated with known clusters. Until some of these funds appear at identifiable exchange addresses, investigators have little visibility into what is happening. Immediate selling pressure also remains unconfirmed.

Ultimately, the Pando Rings exploit remains an active recovery challenge. Continued laundering activity obscures fund movements. However, there are still no confirmed exchange deposits.

The Pando Rings exploiter has resumed laundering funds four years after the attack, moving 4,000 ETH through Tornado Cash.

The renewed activity complicates fund recovery, while 3,548 ETH remain outside the mixer, and no exchange deposits confirm immediate selling.

Near Protocol (NEAR): NEAR is correcting below $4.50, and $4.00 is the key support.

XRP (XRP): XRP stays bearish near $1.25 while it trades below $1.35.

Zcash (ZEC): ZEC is consolidating between $1,150 and $1,350 above its major moving averages.

Shiba Inu (SHIB): SHIB remains bearish near $0.00000464, below every major moving average.

NEAR Protocol is in a short-term bearish correction after its September rally, which took the price from about $1.80 to nearly $5.90 in October. Since that local peak, NEAR has been consolidating, with lower highs and growing selling pressure.

The latest daily candles show bullish momentum fading. NEAR trades around $4.10, below its short-term moving averages. Losing the 4.40–4.50 region matters because it had been acting as dynamic support, and its breakdown suggests sellers are gaining control of the immediate trend.

The wider structure is still constructive, though. NEAR holds above its longer-term moving averages, including the 100-day and 200-day, so the larger bullish trend hasn't been invalidated yet.

The RSI has fallen toward the 40–45 region. Momentum is deteriorating but not oversold, which leaves room for more downside before a meaningful technical recovery.

Key support sits at $4.00, then $3.80 and $3.60. A decisive daily close below $4.00 could speed up selling toward the rising 100-day moving average.

On the upside, NEAR needs to reclaim 4.40–4.50 to stabilize its near-term structure. A sustained breakout above $4.70 would strengthen the recovery case and could open the way to 5.00–5.30.

Outlook: NEAR stays vulnerable to more downside while it trades below $4.50. Holding the 3.80–4.00 support zone could, though, lay the groundwork for renewed accumulation. The broader uptrend is intact, but buyers need to show stronger momentum before a sustainable bullish continuation becomes likely.

XRP's technical structure keeps weakening after its October recovery attempt, which briefly pushed the price toward $1.60. The rejection that followed produced a run of lower highs and lower lows, so bearish momentum has retaken control.

XRP now trades near $1.25, below every major moving average on the daily chart. Short-term and longer-term trend indicators all sit overhead as resistance.

The 1.30–1.35 region matters because it used to be a consolidation area. XRP couldn't hold it, which suggests buyers lack the momentum to reverse the downtrend.

After a sharp decline, the price has stabilized near 1.24–1.25. Volume on the latest candles is fairly modest, though, so there's little sign of aggressive accumulation.

The RSI sits in the lower part of its neutral range, around 35–40, which points to sustained selling pressure. It is approaching oversold territory but hasn't produced a convincing bullish reversal signal.

Immediate support is $1.20, with further downside targets around $1.15 and $1.10. A break below $1.20 could bring another wave of selling.

On the upside, reclaiming $1.30 would be a first improvement, but XRP would need a move above 1.35–1.40 to challenge the bearish structure. The $1.40 area stands out because several moving averages converge nearby.

Outlook: XRP stays bearish in the short term as long as it trades below $1.35. Stabilizing around 1.20–1.25 could prompt a technical rebound, but a real trend reversal needs stronger buying volume and a decisive recovery above key resistance. Until then, rallies remain vulnerable to renewed selling.

Zcash is consolidating after its September rally, which took the price from about $500 to nearly $1,700 in early October. Heavy selling near the peak sent ZEC sharply lower before it settled into a range between $1,150 and $1,350.

On the latest daily candles, buyers are trying to hold the price around $1,250. They have repeatedly failed to sustain momentum above $1,300, so short-term bullish conviction is limited. Neither side has decisive control right now.

Technically, ZEC is above its longer-term moving averages, which keeps the broader bullish structure in place. The rising 100-day and 200-day averages support the underlying uptrend despite the correction.

Shorter-term moving averages are converging around the current range. That suggests volatility compression, which could come before a significant directional move.

The RSI hovers near the neutral 50 level, so momentum is balanced. Volume is down from September's breakout, which points to less speculative participation during the consolidation.

Key support is $1,200, then $1,150 and $1,050. A break below $1,150 would weaken the bullish structure and could speed up losses toward the 100-day moving average.

Immediate resistance is 1,300–1,350. A confirmed breakout above that zone could bring renewed buying, with targets at $1,400 and eventually $1,500.

Outlook: ZEC keeps a cautiously constructive outlook while it holds above 1,150–1,200. Near-term momentum is subdued, but consolidation above the major moving averages suggests the broader uptrend is technically intact. A sustained recovery above $1,350 would give stronger confirmation of bullish continuation.

Shiba Inu remains under sustained bearish pressure after failing to hold its September recovery. A rally toward $0.00000620 gave way to a prolonged correction of progressively lower highs and weakening buying momentum.

The recent decline toward $0.00000450 was followed by a modest attempt to stabilize, but low buying volume suggests demand is still too weak for a convincing reversal.

Immediate support is $0.00000450, then $0.00000430 and $0.00000410. A sustained break below $0.00000450 could bring more selling and a possible retest of its summer lows.

On the upside, initial resistance is 0.00000490–0.00000500. A recovery above $0.00000520 would improve short-term structure, while $0.00000550 remains a significant longer-term resistance level.

Outlook: SHIB keeps a bearish technical outlook while it trades below $0.00000520. The ascending-support breakdown and bearish moving-average positioning suggest downside risks remain elevated. A meaningful recovery would need stronger volume and a sustained reclaim of previously lost support levels.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

#5162. AI agent disputes head to ‘Internet Court’ — and a jury of AI agents

Who better to judge a dispute between AI agents than a jury of different AI models? That’s the idea behind GenLayer, which is already issuing thousands of judgements each day.

The world is headed towards a future in which AI agents will trade stocks and crypto, shop for your family, and much more. But what happens when a bot misinterprets an instruction to buy $10K of “blue chip stocks” and instead buys $10K of Bluebird-brand frozen chips?

Low-level agent disputes like this aren’t suited to the expensive and time-consuming existing legal system. Enter Internet Court, a new protocol that enables agents and humans to enter into contracts, with funds kept in escrow until the terms are judged to have been met.

The dispute resolution mechanism baked into the process employs GenLayer, an open, delegated proof-of-stake network of validators hooked up to AI models.

Operating in beta for the past year, it currently processes around 12,000 decisions per day. It authenticates user-uploaded photos for Collective Memory and resolves arguments on argue.fun. Mainnet is expected to launch next month.

The concept is a modern spin on the famous jury theorem proposed by philosopher Nicolas de Condorcet who argued the probability of reaching the correct answers increases as the number of independent evaluators grows.For Internet Court that means setting up a jury of AI agents from various models in the Claude, Gemini, ChatGPT and DeepSeek families, along with a range of others.

“It’s not making the decision by using a single AI that would have some biases,” explains co-founder and CEO of GenLayer Albert Castellana. “It’s actually using a lot of different AIs, up to 1,500 different AIs. And when that happens, you have essentially better responses.”

“One is left-leaning, the other one is right-leaning. That’s how you get a better answer because one cancels the other.”

Disputes are resolved by sending the details to a randomly selected jury of five GenLayer validators, all running a different AI model. An initial decision is reached within minutes and is based on a simple majority vote, but the decision can be appealed to a wider jury of 11 agents, and then 23, and more and more until you hit the system’s limit.Castellana says finality typically occurs within 30 minutes. “If you wait until maximum finality, it is escalating rounds of appeal. You start with five, and it escalates to fifteen hundred, which could be three hours.”

The Court has 35 members in its open consortium so far, including well-known crypto firms such as ZKsync, NEAR, Consensys and UMA — which resolves disputes for Polymarket.

GenLayer has been in development for almost four years, and began life as an attempt to come up with a better way to evaluate what information is worthy of inclusion in an online knowledge base like Wikipedia.

In theory, anyone is supposed to be able to correct mistakes in the online encyclopedia but in practice, controversial topics from crypto to the war in Gaza get locked, with a small group of powerful editors getting the final say on contested information.

“The original idea behind GenLayer came with […] how could we have a decentralized Wikipedia?” he explains.

Related: Your fridge could one day stake Ether, launch memecoins — CFTC chair

GenLayer enables a new form of smart contract dubbed an “intelligent contract” that allows for subjective judgment. For example, you could contract a freelance writer and allow the system to automatically judge if their content is any good before paying them.

“We have a platform doing exactly this — paying influencers to write content online and then getting the money out,” he explains.

“Intelligent contracts can do code. They can do natural language. They can go to the internet. They can understand an image. They can have judgment,” he says.

The extent to which you can rely on an AI’s judgment in 2026 is limited by the capabilities of the technology. A standup comedian probably wouldn’t hire a joke writer via an intelligent contract.

Castellana explains the tech is also not good enough — yet — to judge whether a picture is a deepfake, or to resolve a boundary dispute between neighbors based on photos. It will certainly give you an answer, but you shouldn’t rely on it just yet. But there are plenty of occasions where AI judgments are already good enough. GenLayer automatically assesses photos uploaded by users to reality mapping platform Collective Memory for quality and accuracy and decides on a payment.

The other big user is Argue.fun, which enables users to bet on the resolution of arguments that are settled by lengthy debates between agents. The top four users have earned more than $2 million each. Most of the questions to date relate to serious policy issues around GenLayer governance, DAOs and AI safety.

Within the next few years, Castellana sees this process paving the way for genuinely autonomous DAOs that can automatically assess proposals according to the DAO’s constitution and wider aims.

GenLayer launches mainnet next month. Source: GenLayer

A more near-term use case is to resolve prediction market disputes more quickly. In mid-2025, Polymarket was sent into a spin over an ambiguously worded $240 million market on whether Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky would wear a suit before July. When he turned up at the June NATO summit wearing an outfit that was vaguely suit-like, the market initially resolved to “Yes.” But after nine days of appeals and back and forth among UMA token holder voters, the decision flipped to “No.”Castellana says GenLayer could shorten that period considerably.”It’s going to be resolved in a matter of minutes, and you will have hundreds, or thousands of AIs that make that vote very, very quickly. So at the end of the day, what you want is a neutral adjudication layer. You want something that is fast, accessible, and cheap.”

Whether humans will accept the decisions is another question entirely. But Internet Court and GenLayer aren’t designed to provide the final judgment, and decisions can still be appealed to humans.”After that, if you are still disagreeing and still think that Zelensky is not wearing a suit, what you can do is basically take it to Kleros, UMA, or even to a human court.”

Outsourcing dispute resolution to AI models comes with some big risks. The first is that the technology is just too immature to produce reliable judgments.

Another problem is that AI models reproduce many of the biases they find in the training data — and to date, the tech firms’ attempts to redress those biases have sometimes just flipped the bias in the other direction.

A third potential issue is a phenomenon described in an MIT paper called Representational Convergence. The paper suggests that over time, AI models from different companies all start to converge onto a similar statistical model of reality. If that’s true, then in five years Internet Court’s AI jurors could end up with a severe case of groupthink.

Castellana is not convinced this theory is valid. “I see many more models existing rather than fewer,” he says. “I see endless models coming from every country, every company, and every team. I cannot imagine that all of them will just converge into the exact same thing.”

#5161. Ripple Partner Reaches One Million People With Safe Water

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple Partner Reaches One Million People With Safe Water

The access to clean water isn’t taken for granted in many jurisdictions across the globe. Water. Org is here to fix it.

Ripple and Water.org are expanding a collaboration that has already helped more than one million people gain access to safe water or sanitation financing across Latin America.

The nonprofit, co-founded by Gary White and Matt Damon, is now layering Ripple Payments, the RLUSD stablecoin, and digital asset custody tools into its operations so capital can move faster and cheaper into emerging markets.

Water.org has used Ripple Payments to move more than $3.2 million across four markets.

Settlement times in Mexico and Brazil dropped from two to three days to same-day, while payment processing became available around the clock instead of being limited by a single daily banking cutoff.

Transaction fees fell by as much as 66 percent, so a larger share of every dollar reaches borrowers rather than intermediaries.

Ripple committed $6 million over three years to support Water.org programs such as WaterCredit, which partners with local financial institutions to offer affordable loans for household taps, toilets, and piped connections.

Finance as a tool to solve the water crisis 💧@Water co-founders Matt Damon and Gary White joined @bgarlinghouse on the Swell main stage to share how Ripple Payments and RLUSD are helping @Water reach more people, faster. pic.twitter.com/VXdttesBK6

Separately, Water.org received a $1.25 million grant in Ripple’s RLUSD through the Stablecoin Impact Fund. That capital is intended to expand stablecoin-enabled WaterCredit disbursements into Indonesia, the Philippines, and Uganda.

Water.org will also begin using Ripple’s Wallet-as-a-Service technology to manage digital assets as the programs scale. Beyond the direct funding, Ripple joined Get Blue, the Water.org movement modeled after the (RED) campaign, as its exclusive digital-asset and fintech partner.

Founding brand partners include Gap, Amazon, Starbucks, and Ecolab. Ripple’s first year of Get Blue support helped reach 400,000 people, with the three-year commitment expected to support 1.2 million as Water.org works toward its broader goal of reaching 200 million people by 2030.

Gary White, co-founder and CEO of Water.org, said the actual water crisis is fundamentally a funding-flow problem rather than a lack of capital, and that Ripple’s infrastructure has shown what efficient movement of money looks like in practice.

Brad Garlinghouse, Ripple’s CEO, noted that the partnership demonstrates the original promise of crypto and blockchain: making money move faster, more efficiently, and globally for real-world needs.

💧🌍 WATER IS LIFE. CRYPTO IS CHANGING THE GAME! 🚀Hollywood superstar Matt Damon in conversation with @bgarlinghouse is helping tackle the global water crisis through https://t.co/AMHUwOVte1, and Ripple is part of a bigger conversation about using financial innovation to… pic.twitter.com/U42l8YvnZM

White and Matt Damon joined Garlinghouse for a fireside chat at Ripple Swell in New York.

The expanded setup combines traditional philanthropic capital with on-chain settlement and custody so that repaid loans can be recycled into new lending through vehicles such as the WaterEquity Everspring Fund.

For Water.org, the practical result is more of each donated or invested dollar reaching families that need household water connections. For Ripple, it is another demonstration of its payments and stablecoin stack operating outside pure trading venues and inside humanitarian finance.

#5160. What is EIP-7702? The Ethereum Upgrade Behind the $6.4B Binance Hack

What is EIP-7702? The Ethereum Upgrade Behind the $6.4B Binance Hack

Ethereum has two kinds of accounts. Externally owned accounts (EOAs), the standard wallets most people use, are controlled by a private key and can do little more than send transactions. Smart contract accounts can run code, enabling features like spending limits, account recovery and paying fees in tokens other than ETH, but most users never moved to them.

EIP-7702 is an attempt to bridge that gap. Instead of asking users to migrate to new wallets, it lets existing EOAs borrow the code of a smart contract while keeping their address and funds.

This guide explains what EIP-7702 is, how it works, why developers wanted it, and how attackers used it in the largest crypto theft on record.

EIP-7702, titled "Set Code for EOAs," is an Ethereum Improvement Proposal written in May 2024 by Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin and developers Sam Wilson, Ansgar Dietrichs and lightclient, according to the official specification.

It was one of the headline changes in Pectra, a major Ethereum upgrade that went live on May 7, 2025.

"EIP-7702 lays the foundation we've needed for a long time," Ethereum core developer Preston Van Loon told Decrypt at the time. "While full abstraction won't be immediately available to end users with Pectra, this upgrade gives Ethereum the infrastructure to support it moving forward."

EIP-7702 is a change to Ethereum's core protocol, not a token or a separate blockchain, so there is nothing to buy.

EIP-7702 adds a new type of Ethereum transaction, the "set code transaction." It carries a list of authorizations, each signed with the private key of the account being upgraded, that names the address of a smart contract the account wants to use.

Once the transaction is processed, the account stores a small pointer, called a delegation indicator, to that contract. From then on, whenever anyone calls the account, Ethereum runs the contract's code as if it belonged to the account, with full access to its funds.

The delegation lasts until the account owner signs a new authorization, either pointing to a different contract or clearing the pointer to restore a plain EOA.

EIP-7702 marks delegated accounts using the byte 0xef, a code value that was banned in earlier upgrades so it could be reserved for special purposes like this.

The specification names three main features it was designed to deliver:

Those same features carry risks. Because delegated code gets "unrestricted access to the account," apps should never ask users to sign an EIP-7702 authorization, and wallets should not offer an interface for it, the specification warns. "There is no safe way to provide this interface," it says.

Scammers moved quickly. Within weeks of Pectra's launch, trading firm Wintermute found that more than 80% of EIP-7702 delegations pointed to copies of the same malicious "sweeper" contract, which automatically drains wallets whose keys have leaked, The Block reported in June 2025.

On October 29, 2026, an attacker moved some $6.4 billion out of Binance wallets on 10 blockchains, most of it within about 20 minutes. Blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis both attributed the theft to TraderTraitor, the North Korean hacking group behind the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025.

On Ethereum, the attacker used an EIP-7702 delegation to turn a Binance hot wallet into a sweeper it controlled, with 50 authorizations signed in advance, according to TRM Labs. An operational wallet funded through crypto mixer Tornado Cash submitted the signed authorizations, which let the hot wallet run attacker-deployed code, and the attacker then called the wallet to sweep its assets into addresses it controlled, Chainalysis said.

Crucially, EIP-7702 delegations require a valid signature from the account being upgraded. Binance wallets on the other nine chains also sent funds to the attacker through ordinary signed transfers, which TRM said points to a compromise of Binance's hot wallet signing infrastructure rather than a flaw in Ethereum itself.

Binance has said its investigation began with a compromised engineer's laptop, and has since found "additional points of breach."

EIP-7702 is a first step toward full account abstraction, a long-standing goal of making every Ethereum account as flexible as a smart contract. Its authors designed it to be forward-compatible with that "endgame," so wallets built on it today should keep working as Ethereum's account model evolves.

The next step could come with Hegotá, the upgrade planned to follow Glamsterdam on Ethereum's roadmap. In February 2026, Buterin said that EIP-8141, which he called "an omnibus that wraps up and solves every remaining problem" account abstraction was meant to address, looked "possible to make happen within a year" in the Hegotá fork.

We have been talking about account abstraction ever since early 2016, see the original EIP-86: https://t.co/HYLSTLHgWH

Now, we finally have EIP-8141 ( https://t.co/jYqeS55j6P ), an omnibus that wraps up and solves every remaining problem that AA was…

— vitalik.eth (@VitalikButerin) February 28, 2026

"We have been talking about account abstraction ever since early 2016," Buterin wrote, adding that developers are also discussing whether existing accounts could join the new framework, which would give them batching and sponsored transactions natively.

It is part of a wider rebuild. In July 2026, Buterin described a new draft of Ethereum's long-term roadmap as the network's biggest overhaul since the Merge, its 2022 switch to proof of stake.

The Federal Bureau of Investigation is urging private entities, including the node operators of THORChain [RUNE], RPC node operators, instant exchanges, DeFi firms, and blockchain analytics firms, to “block transactions with or derived from addresses (of) TraderTraitor actors.”

A reorg happens when the network replaces a recent block with a competing block. Node operators halted all chains as a precaution so developers could investigate. This inadvertently delayed the North Korean hackers.

Then revenue: 85% ($1.2B) of the Bybit hack was washed into BTC via THORChain. Nodes, LPs and front-ends collected $12M in fees from the Bybit hack. THORChain has to pick whether it is a protocol or an exchange.

A drop into the $0.41-$0.50 long-term demand zone can be expected next.

#5158. Binance Restores Withdrawals for Ripple's RLUSD Stablecoin

Cryptocurrency exchange Binance has fully enabled withdrawals for Ripple’s RLUSD stablecoin on the Ethereum network. This move is part of the exchange's gradual service recovery process after the massive $6.3 billion hack.

On Nov. 3, the exchange released an update on the next stage of its withdrawal restoration plan, with a new list of cryptocurrencies available for withdrawal.

Interestingly, the new list of assets includes Ripple’s US dollar-backed stablecoin RLUSD and a number of other stablecoins.

This announcement follows Binance’s gradual restoration of withdrawals, which began on Nov. 1 after a brief halt due to one of the largest hacks in the crypto industry.

As per the latest recovery update from Binance, the exchange began its second significant withdrawal restoration phase at 04:00 UTC on Nov. 3.

Apart from RLUSD, the cryptocurrency exchange is now gradually re-enabling withdrawals for dozens of cryptocurrencies on multiple blockchain networks, including Ethereum, BNB Smart Chain, Solana, XRP Ledger, Sui, Arbitrum, Optimism, Tron and Base.

For Ethereum, the list of cryptocurrencies now available for withdrawal includes Shiba Inu (SHIB), Chainlink (LINK), Worldcoin (WLD), Ondo (ONDO) and Ethena (ENA).

At the same time, the cryptocurrency exchange has also added XRP and a number of other top crypto assets to its list of withdrawals that will soon be re-enabled on BNB Smart Chain.

The crypto exchange emphasized that not all of the listed assets will be enabled for withdrawal at once. Instead, each asset and network combination will be re-enabled individually after the necessary security and operational checks are completed.

“We will gradually re-enable more assets & networks in stages once we complete the relevant security checks,” Binance said.

At the same time, the crypto exchange also warned that there may be delays due to traffic during this restoration process. Meanwhile, cryptocurrency deposits remain functional.

Initially, Binance started re-enabling withdrawals on Nov. 1 for top cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), XRP, Solana (SOL), Dogecoin (DOGE) and Zcash (ZEC), as well as major stablecoins USDT and USDC.

The latest withdrawal restoration update comes only a few days after the cryptocurrency exchange suffered a massive hack on Oct. 29, which resulted in the theft of about $6.3 billion in crypto.

The FBI officially identified the perpetrators of the hack as North Korean hackers linked to the infamous TraderTraitor operation on Oct. 31.

The U.S. agency revealed that hackers have been converting stolen cryptocurrencies mostly into Ethereum as a potential path toward laundering the assets through decentralized finance platforms and crypto swap services.

More withdrawal restorations will follow as Binance moves forward with its security checks and recovery processes.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

#5156. XRP’s Security Fix Puts Validator Trust Under Scrutiny

Home » Trendsetter News » XRP’s Security Fix Puts Validator Trust Under Scrutiny

An XRP Ledger security patch released before full code disclosure has sparked debate over vulnerability management and validator influence.

A security patch released before its full code was made public is at the center of a dispute over XRP Ledger governance. In a video addressing the controversy, Kamilah Stevenson, a finance commentator and longtime XRP holder, argues that the restricted disclosure protected the network rather than substantiated allegations of fraud.

The criticism came from Cyber Capital fund manager Justin Bonds, who, according to Stevenson, challenged both the upgrade process and the influence of recommended validator lists. The dispute raises two distinct questions for investors: how developers handle dangerous vulnerabilities, and how much discretion network operators retain.

Stevenson says a researcher reported an overflow vulnerability through the XRP Ledger’s bug bounty program on September 22. The flaw, which she says had existed since around 2015, potentially allowed an attacker to obtain XRP improperly or create unauthorized tokens.

According to her account, developers released version 3.4.1 on September 25 without immediately publishing the full fix code. More than 80% of main validators upgraded that day, and an amendment activated on October 9, leaving nodes without the update unable to remain synchronized.

Dr. Stevenson reports that a subsequent ledger audit found no exploitation or unauthorized XRP creation. The transcript does not identify the auditor or provide the underlying report.

She compares the response with Bitcoin Core’s handling of a 2018 vulnerability: developers initially disclosed a crash risk, withholding its inflation implications until users had time to upgrade.

“You protect the network first and you explain second,” she says. That comparison supports delayed vulnerability disclosure, though it does not by itself resolve concerns about inspecting the XRP update.

Stevenson acknowledges that publishers of recommended Unique Node Lists wield influence because many operators adopt those defaults. Operators can choose their own lists, she says, while Ripple runs only one validator on the most commonly used list.

She also points to the amendment threshold: more than 80% validator support sustained for two weeks. Those safeguards challenge the idea that Ripple can unilaterally rewrite ledger rules, but validator ownership alone does not settle questions about influence over defaults.

In a nutshell, the promised code release and technical write-up would offer firmer grounds for judging this episode than either a fraud allegation or assurances from an XRP holder.

#5155. Coinbase Data Shows XRP May See 8.7% Price Move

Options market data for cryptocurrencies published by major US crypto exchange Coinbase indicates that a big (approximately 8.7%) price swing for XRP could be expected by Nov. 8.

At the time of writing, XRP and Solana are tied in terms of the biggest expected price move (according to options-implied volatility), both showing an 8.7% expected move.

Ethereum (ETH) has an expected 7.7% move. Meanwhile, Bitcoin (BTC) is the asset with the lowest expected price move (according to implied volatility) among the top four assets (5.8%).

It is important to note that the above numbers do not necessarily mean that a big upward move in the price of XRP is expected.

They only mean that options traders are expecting significant moves (up or down).

According to Coinbase data, an 8.7% price move (up or down) could be possible within the specified timeframe.

The current price of XRP is around $1.25.

An 8.7% move up would mean the price of XRP could reach around $1.36.

An 8.7% move down would mean the price of XRP could reach around $1.14.

That would mean the implied price range for XRP is around 1.14–1.36.

It is worth noting that Coinbase's numbers are based on "implied volatility," which is a measure based on options prices that reflects the market's expectations for future price swings.

Coinbase is referring to a "one standard deviation" or 1σ move. In the typical normal distribution context, this means there's about a 68% chance that the asset will end up in the specified range.

That does not mean XRP will necessarily be in that price range, nor that there's a 50/50 chance of an 8.7% rally or fall.

What it means is that the options market provides an estimate of the size of the expected move, but it does not necessarily indicate the direction of the move.

Would appreciate if you show a little support. Thanks!

😂 did say anything about selling SPCX shares, was just saying/showing some funds that he might know, so that he can be less worried.

— CZ 🔶 BNB (@cz_binance) October 30, 2026

The argument comes as Binance deals with the largest cryptocurrency theft on record. At least $6.4 billion in crypto was taken from wallets associated with the exchange on Thursday, October 29, across multiple blockchains.

Just yesterday, initial reports showed that at least $5.57 billion had left Binance-linked wallets across Ethereum, Bitcoin, and Tron. The attackers moved funds through several transactions, including a transfer of more than $1.6 billion in USDT to a newly created Ethereum address.

The incident also exposed an important weakness in how cryptocurrency can be recovered after a theft. Tether and Circle, the companies behind the USDT and USDC stablecoins, froze approximately $1.8 billion in assets associated with the attackers in the initial response, then subsequently froze a total of $2.39 billion. Unlike those tokens, Ethereum cannot be frozen by a central issuer, making it harder to stop stolen funds once they are converted.

The attackers subsequently used decentralized exchanges such as Uniswap and lending protocol Aave to move funds into Ethereum. These platforms allow users to trade or borrow cryptocurrency without relying on a traditional intermediary to approve each transaction. Decrypt tracked wallets receiving tens of thousands of ETH through these operations, including 19,762 ETH borrowed against approximately $60 million in USDT.

The suspected attackers have also been linked to North Korea. In a Friday report, blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis attributed the attack to TraderTraitor, a North Korean hacking group previously blamed for the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025. Chainalysis estimated the Binance theft at approximately $6.4 billion.

Binance has since said it has reserved more than $6.34 billion in company funds to cover the incident, including $5 billion already held in the same tokens that were stolen. The exchange said it is acquiring the remaining assets through market and over-the-counter purchases, which can take longer when certain tokens have limited liquidity.

#5153. Strategy spends triple its weekly Bitcoin outlay on STRC buybacks

Strategy spent $208.2 million buying back STRC preferred shares and $65.5 million on Bitcoin, while allocating $98.5 million to its USD Cash balance.

Michael Saylor’s Strategy spent more than three times as much buying back its STRC preferred shares as it did acquiring Bitcoin last week.

Strategy repurchased $208.2 million in STRC and spent about $65.5 million buying 811 Bitcoin (BTC) at an average price of $80,846, according to a Monday 8-K filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

Strategy sold about 1.18 million MSTR shares for net proceeds of $164 million. About $65.5 million funded its Bitcoin acquisition, while the remaining $98.5 million was allocated to the company’s USD Cash balance, according to the filing. The company held $132.7 million in USD Cash and $4.79 billion in its separate US dollar reserve as of Sunday.

The latest purchases followed about $664 million in STRC buybacks between Oct. 1 and Oct. 25, compared with about $72 million in Bitcoin acquisitions over that period.

Strategy’s STRC preferred stock rose 0.1% in pre-market trading on Monday, to $99.22, or about 0.8% below its $100 target price, according to Yahoo Finance. Strategy’s policy is not to issue new STRC shares below $100, limiting that source of funding while the stock trades below its target.

Another Bitcoin treasury company, Japan’s Metaplanet, reported buybacks of its common shares on Monday. It spent about 25.27 billion yen ($160 million) to buy back 96.45 million shares between Oct. 14 and Saturday, representing roughly 7.1% of its issued shares.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Arbitrum [ARB] was down 4.5% in the past 24 hours, following a market-wide sell-off as Bitcoin [BTC] prices slipped below $77k once again.

The ARB price slide below the $0.162 local low was a blow for swing traders and heralded further losses. The MACD was on the verge of a bearish crossover just above the zero line on the weekly chart.

However, like the swing structure, the OBV has also been trending higher in recent months. Barring a massive sell-off, ARB prices staying above $0.11 is enough to keep long-term investors’ bias comfortably bullish.

The Arbitrum Security Council froze 8,425 Ethereum [ETH] and 74.555 million ARB, worth $30 million, on the 30th of October. The Security Council voted to execute an emergency action that moved the funds out of an address that was identified in the Binance exploit.

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Tight-Band Squeeze Puts XRP On Alert for a Violent Break

XRP’s price readies a hard move as Ali Martinez identifies a long-unseen Bollinger Band Squeeze.

XRP’s price is currently trading in a no-trade zone, according to veteran market analyst Ali Martinez. Currently, the 4-hour XRP price charts present a setup where the Bollinger Bands (BOLL) have squeezed to their narrowest levels since August, 2026.

Last time a similar compression occurred, XRP’s price soared by 71% in 72 hours, but a Bollinger Band squeeze doesn’t necessarily predict price direction, Ali says. A 4-hour candlestick close above $1.27 would restore bullish dominance, while below $1.23 constitutes a bearish trend continuing.

XRP: A BIG MOVE IS COMINGThe Bollinger Bands on the 4-hour chart have squeezed to their narrowest levels since August.The last time they were this compressed, $XRP rallied 71% in just three days.But a squeeze doesn’t predict direction.That’s why I’m watching:• 4H close… pic.twitter.com/Dy2vRNlXWZ

Meanwhile, other fundamental on-chain metrics painted a bearish near-term picture. The whale sentiment-measuring Chaikin Money Flow (CMF) stood at -0.38, still a heavy distribution among big-time investors. The Relative Strength Index (RSI) plunged to oversold levels, which could help a bullish reversal if met with proper trading volume. Another crucial indicator that gives hope for XRP’s bulls is the Bull Bear Power (BBP) shifting towards slightly positive levels, dwelling in the green territory after a 20-day hiatus.

The volume on XRP’s Futures trading has skyrocketed by 88.20%, now topping $1.95 billion, according to CoinGlass. The Open Interest, otherwise known as unsettled plays, have also spiked slightly.

As short-sellers attempt to push XRP below $1.23, the next major demand zone for XRP lies at $1, according to the Bollinger Bands on the one-day charts.

If XRP’s price secures the $1.27 tag on the 4-hour time-frame, the next target is set at $1.34, representing the mid-tier Bollinger Band. Going upward, the next strong resistance area circles around $1.67, a price area unclaimed in months.

With Bitcoin (BTC) testing the lower support boundary at $76,770, XRP’s high price correlation may create an additional hurdle.

#5149. FBI blames North Korea for the $6.3B Binance hack – Kim Yo Jong slams back

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The $6.3 billion Binance hack has drawn international attention after the FBI blames North Korea as responsible for the 29th October theft.

The agency attributed the attack to TraderTraitor, a state-sponsored hacking group. Kim Yo Jong, Kim Jong-un’s sister, accused the United States of using fabrications to create the U.S. accusations against North Korea for its alleged theft.

This latest development has taken the focus off the hack itself, putting the spotlight back onto the issue of who was responsible for it.

The FBI is also examining blockchain links to previous hacks, including the $1.5 billion Bybit attack, to verify its suspicions.

As a result of this possible rift in the investigation process, recovery of stolen assets, along with establishing accountability, will likely be even more complicated.

Beyond the diplomatic dispute, the movement of the stolen funds provides another way to assess the FBI’s attribution. Blockchain analytics firms have traced the assets across 12,000 wallets and more than 40,000 transactions on 11 blockchains.

Furthermore, according to blockchain analytics firms, many of the Binance proceeds have been laundered through TRON [TRX]. This is by rapidly transferring funds between various addresses, repeatedly cycling the funds through various addresses.

Other methods used in the laundering process include cross-chain bridges, DeFi swaps, and very little usage of mixers. These connections can provide insight to investigators as to what might constitute a common network among hackers conducting similar hacks.

However, tracking movement of funds does allow investigators to see how funds were laundered. Instead, investigators would need additional evidence to show who specifically controlled which addresses.

Currently, investigators face a harder task of transitioning from tracing stolen funds to identifying those responsible.

While blockchain records indicate how Binance hacked funds moved about in terms of laundering and money laundering, they do not indicate who was ultimately directing the hack.

Therefore, investigators will need to determine if any of the wallets are connected to the previous work of North Korea-related groups. Possible sources of data that can aid investigators in determining this include malware samples, server logs, and IP addresses.

Meanwhile, exchange records may reveal where the attackers attempted to convert or withdraw the stolen assets. These findings could turn the FBI’s attribution into a stronger case for accountability.

Without independent forensic evidence and international cooperation, however, proving direct state involvement remains difficult.

The FBI blamed North Korea for the $6.3 billion Binance hack, but Pyongyang denies it.

Blockchain trails reveal widespread laundering, but North Korea’s role remains unproven.

Muriuki Lazaro is a on-chain data analyst with a B.Sc. in Data Science. Muriuki specializes in dissecting complex on-chain data into clear and accurate insights for readers in the crypto ecosystem, with a particular focus on Bitcoin.

#5148. Evernorth’s Warrants Could Raise $135 Million.. Or Not

Home » Trendsetter News » Evernorth’s Warrants Could Raise $135 Million.. Or Not

With millions of shares becoming tradable, financing terms & decisions will shape the company’s ability to expand holdings.

Evernorth could receive roughly $135 million from outstanding warrants, or nothing, depending on how they are exercised. In a new crypto research, a popular analyst examining the XRP treasury company’s financing argues that this distinction matters more than its stock’s reported one-day collapse of roughly 50%.

The company holds about 473 million XRP, according to Fire Hustle. Its ability to expand that position depends partly on its share price, financing terms and whether investors decide to sell newly tradable stock.

Fire Hustle identifies approximately 11.7 million warrants, each carrying an $11.50 exercise price. Full exercise for cash would bring Evernorth roughly $135 million, potentially funding additional XRP purchases.

A sustained share price of at least $18 – on 20 trading days within a 30-trading-day period – would allow the company to call the warrants, subject to the terms described in the video. Holders would then face a deadline to exercise before the warrants were redeemed for a nominal amount.

But management could require cashless exercise instead. That would produce fewer new shares while bringing in no exercise proceeds. “The option that Evernorth picks,” Fire Hustle says, determines whether this financing route delivers fresh purchasing power for XRP.

The distinction is important: a rising stock price alone would not guarantee another large XRP purchase.

According to the YouTube video, cash investors including SBI Holdings could sell more than 13 million shares once the relevant resale registration becomes effective.

Fire Hustle says those investors did not sign the six-month lockups covering Ripple, a Larsen family trust and Arrington’s fund.

That potential supply is substantial beside the fewer than five million shares the analyst says remained publicly held after heavy redemptions by the blank-check company’s investors.

Still, eligibility to sell is not evidence of an imminent exit. The analyst estimates SBI paid in the mid-to-high teens per share and notes its longstanding relationship with Ripple, suggesting less incentive to sell near its entry price.

A premium to those assets can support further purchases; a discount can undermine that strategy and make buybacks more attractive.

Fire Hustle raises XRP sales as a possible consequence, not an announced company plan. Investors therefore have two distinct developments to watch: when restricted shares become tradable, and whether warrant exercises actually put cash into Evernorth.

#5147. Binance Sets Aside $6.34B to Cover Hack Losses, CEO Says 'Your Assets Are Safe'

Binance Sets Aside $6.34B to Cover Hack Losses, CEO Says 'Your Assets Are Safe'

Binance will cover every dollar lost in this week's hack with its own money, CEO Richard Teng said in a video address on Friday, adding that customer funds were never touched.

"We are committed to covering all hacked losses through our company funds. Your assets are safe," Teng said, according to a transcript he tweeted.

An update on the security incident. pic.twitter.com/qn2804fO5z

— Richard Teng (@_RichardTeng) October 30, 2026

Binance has reserved more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover the stolen assets, $5 billion of which is already held in the exact tokens taken, including USDC, USDT, BTC, ETH and BNB, he said. The exchange is buying the rest through market and over-the-counter channels.

Teng said the hack hit Binance's own hot wallet, not customer funds. "We never use any customer funds, so they are not impacted," he said, adding that most user assets are held in cold wallets and only moved into a hot wallet when a user withdraws. Binance's ability to absorb a loss of this size comes from keeping much of its operating earnings in crypto, he said.

Binance had earlier said it would cover all losses from its SAFU emergency fund and company funds.

Teng said the hack "was not carried out by one person." Binance initially traced it to a compromised laptop but has since found additional points of breach. The attacker also used a self-destruct mechanism that destroyed tooling and traces along the way, making the attack harder to investigate, Teng said.

The attacker used EIP-7702, an Ethereum feature that lets standard wallets run smart contract code, to obtain onchain execution approval over a hot wallet, according to Teng. "That delegation did not pass through our normal approval controls," he said, which is why Binance paused withdrawals to rebuild and retest its infrastructure. Blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis have attributed the theft to North Korean hacking group TraderTraitor.

Binance hopes to restore withdrawals before its Sunday deadline, Teng said, and is carrying out a full security review covering endpoints, cloud, key management and signing. It plans to publish a full proof of reserves report by Monday, with an initial view before then.

The attacker is still moving funds. Between 11:37 a.m. and 12:12 p.m. ET on Friday, a Tron wallet that Arkham attributes to the hacker sent about 5.19 million TRX, worth roughly $1.73 million, to 12 addresses, according to Arkham data.

All markets are now unfrozen across Aave V3 and V4.

They were frozen as a precaution after initial reports, which paused new deposits and borrows while withdrawals and repayments continued as normal.

Decentralized lending protocol Aave, where the attacker borrowed ETH against USDT while racing stablecoin freezes, according to TRM Labs, said on Friday that it had unfrozen all markets on its V3 and V4 deployments. It had frozen them as a precaution after the first reports of the hack, pausing new deposits and borrowing while withdrawals and repayments continued. "Aave was unaffected, and all positions remain overcollateralized," founder Stani Kulechov wrote.

Binance co-founder Changpeng "CZ" Zhao said that while the $6.3 billion theft is the largest in dollar terms, it is not the largest in Bitcoin terms, pointing to Mt. Gox's loss of 650,000 BTC, more than 5% of the supply. Unlike at Mt. Gox and FTX, where nearly all users were affected, Binance is covering the losses itself, he said. "It hurts, but we survive," Zhao wrote.

#5146. XRP Defies Bear Market: New Wallets Double Despite Price Pressure

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The XRP Ledger network recorded the largest capital migration in recent months. According to the Santiment platform, on Sunday, November 1, users created 7,934 new wallets. This is the sixth-largest daily result in all of 2026, which, moreover, occurred on a weekend — traditionally the most passive period for blockchain networks.

The influx of new addresses more than doubled the autumn average of 3,500 wallets.

The surge in activity was a direct reaction to the hack of the Binance exchange on October 29. During the attack on the platform’s hot wallets, hackers, whom the FBI links to the North Korean group TraderTraitor, stole assets worth $6.36 billion. Also falling into their hands were 233 million XRP tokens (about $297 million).

Instead of panic selling, investors began saving their capital. The mass opening of addresses is a consequence of withdrawing tokens from the centralized platform to independent “cold” wallets (self-custody). Investors are taking control of their private keys and distributing risks by transferring coins to clean, newly created addresses.

This behavior reflects growing distrust of centralized custodians, as even the largest exchanges remain vulnerable to sophisticated attacks.

Despite the fact that a huge volume of XRP ended up under the hackers’ control, the market held up. The price found strong support at the $1.20 level and is now consolidating around $1.25. Such resilience suggests that holders view the incident as an exchange-specific problem rather than a threat to the XRP Ledger itself.

A rare precedent has emerged on the market: XRP’s on-chain metrics are growing despite external stress and a price lull. If the influx of new wallets holds above 4,000 per day, buyers will have a strong foundation for betting on a rise.

The first signal for growth will be the price consolidating above the $1.30 resistance. If, however, network optimism fades and daily growth falls below 3,000 addresses, the asset will return to a prolonged flat phase.

#5143. Animoca Brands clears final hurdle for Abu Dhabi fund management

The former mobile games company is building out its fund management business in Abu Dhabi as it targets institutional demand for stablecoins and tokenized assets.

Animoca Brands said Monday it had secured approval to manage investment funds for non-retail clients in Abu Dhabi, expanding the asset management business of a company that began in mobile games before moving into blockchain gaming and venture investing.

The authorization, granted to subsidiary Animoca Asset Management (AD) Limited by Abu Dhabi Global Market’s Financial Services Regulatory Authority (FSRA), allows it to oversee funds that pool money from professional investors rather than the general public.

The approval announced Monday took effect on Oct. 13, bringing a process that began with in-principle approval in November 2025 to completion. In the intervening months, Animoca also secured a Dubai license in February to provide virtual asset brokerage and investment management services to institutional and qualified investors.

Animoca Brands was spun off from mobile game developer Animoca in 2014, initially developing and publishing games based on licensed brands alongside original titles. It shifted its focus toward blockchain in 2018 and built an investment portfolio that included OpenSea, Dapper Labs and Axie Infinity developer Sky Mavis.

In a September investor update, the company described a broader transition from a digital asset and Web3 operator into a regulated financial services institution operating across multiple jurisdictions, amid a broader push by crypto-native companies into traditional financial services. Under that strategy, digital assets are taking on a more institutional role in its business, with a focus on stablecoins, real-world asset tokenization and treasury management.

#5142. Mercado Bitcoin hack: Attacker still holds PEPE, AAVE, and PENDLE as millions remain exposed

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

A major security breach occurred on the Mercado Bitcoin exchange. Hackers stole about $46 million in cryptocurrencies. After the exploit, investigators were able to track all the stolen money through 65 different addresses.

The most significant amount was held in an Ethereum [ETH] account valued at about $8.26 million.

Bitcoin [BTC] trailed closely with 106.068 BTC, worth approximately $8.1 million.

The wallets also contain $5.03 million in Tether [USDT] and $3.03 million in USD Coin [USDC]. Meanwhile, more than $9 million in stolen assets have been frozen.

However, the hacker’s remaining balances leave several tokens exposed to further transfers, laundering attempts, and potential selling pressure.

Although investigators have frozen more than $9 million, the attacker still controls substantial altcoin balances. Because the attacker can no longer access much of the stolen funds due to the frozen assets.

However, this does create the potential for another type of market risk. Instead, this shifts the focus to where the attacker ultimately transfers those tokens, whether to an exchange or one or more DeFi platforms.

PePe [PEPE], Aave [AAVE], and Pendle [PENDLE] each represent more than 80% of that total and carry the most risk since they would be transferred into relatively illiquid areas of the market.

The values of those tokens are approximately $1.79 million, $1.61 million, and $1.39 million, respectively.

The attacker also appears to hold very significant positions in Uniswap [UNI] and Ondo [ONDO]. If such tokens are sent to trading venues, large sell orders could overwhelm existing liquidity.

As such, could result in even steeper declines in thinner markets while causing even greater uncertainty for token holders who may expect subsequent transfers.

The key risk now lies in how easily the hacker could sell the remaining tokens without disrupting their markets. Despite PEPE and AAVE having higher token balances being stolen from them, each platform has greater than $230 million in daily trading volume.

Therefore, a concentrated sale could potentially overwhelm existing liquidity. In turn, this would lead to steeper declines in price than if that same sale had taken place in a token with deeper liquidity.

Meanwhile, the absence of confirmed exchange deposits suggests the hacker has not yet begun large-scale liquidation. However, any movement toward trading platforms could increase volatility, particularly for PENDLE, where market depth offers less protection.

The Mercado Bitcoin hack involved approximately $46 million in stolen cryptocurrency, with investigators freezing over $9 million.

The attacker retains millions in PEPE, AAVE, PENDLE, and other altcoins, leaving their markets exposed to potential sell-offs.

#5141. Social Security November Payments: Why Dates Shifted This Month

Home » Blitz news » Social Security November Payments: Why Dates Shifted This Month

November’s payment calendar includes an early SSI deposit, a Veterans Day adjustment and four scheduled Social Security distribution dates.

Millions of Americans receiving Social Security and Supplemental Security Income (SSI) benefits face an adjusted payment schedule this November. Due to a federal holiday and calendar shifts, several payment groups will receive their deposits earlier than expected.

Crucially, Supplemental Security Income (SSI) recipients will not receive a payment in November, as their monthly distribution was issued on Friday, October 30.

The Social Security Administration (SSA) distributes regular monthly benefits across four specific dates in November:

The primary schedule shift in November involves Veterans Day, observed on Wednesday, November 11.

Because federal offices and banking institutions close for federal holidays, beneficiaries born between the 1st and 10th—who normally receive funds on the second Wednesday of the month—will have their direct deposits issued one day early on Tuesday, November 10.

All subsequent birthday groups (Nov. 18 and Nov. 25) will receive payments on their regular Wednesday schedule.

Federal law mandates electronic payment via direct deposit or the Direct Express Debit Mastercard. Most deposits post by 9:00 a.m. local time on the payment date. If an expected check is missing, beneficiaries are advised to contact their financial institution before calling the SSA helpline.

November’s payment schedule is not the only development beneficiaries should watch.

In addition to November payment updates, the SSA has finalized a 3.6% cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) for 2027.

The 3.6% adjustment raises average monthly retirement checks by approximately $75 per month starting in January 2027. SSI recipients will receive their updated January 2027 payment early on December 31, 2026, as January 1 is a federal holiday.

Beneficiaries with personal my Social Security accounts will also be able to access their personalized COLA notices online starting in late November. Paper notices are expected to arrive in December.

Social Security and SSI payments are a steady source of cash flow for millions of Americans. Knowing the exact dates helps recipients plan spending, including any money they allocate to investments.
