#5140. Secret Service, FBI Join Binance Hack Probe as Circle Freezes $823 Million

Secret Service, FBI Join Binance Hack Probe as Circle Freezes $823 Million

Binance is working with the FBI and U.S. Secret Service after hackers stole $6.4 billion from the cryptocurrency exchange, as Circle joins the recovery effort by freezing more than $800 million in assets connected to the attack, according to Reuters.

A person familiar with the matter told Reuters that Binance was cooperating with law enforcement agencies around the world, including both U.S. agencies. The FBI confirmed it was aware of the incident and was working with public and private partners to assess its scope and coordinate investigative steps. The Secret Service remained silent.

Investigating financial crimes is part of the Secret Service’s job, beyond its better-known responsibility of protecting the president. The agency also investigates computer crimes, unauthorized access to computer systems, and crimes involving digital currencies.

Circle, the company behind USDC, a stablecoin designed to maintain a value of $1, has separately confirmed that it is coordinating with law enforcement. CEO Jeremy Allaire said the company had frozen more than $800 million in USDC associated with the Binance incident and would continue supporting the investigation.

Tether, which issues the competing USDT stablecoin, froze another $1.5 billion in tokens. Both companies can blacklist specific blockchain addresses, preventing the tokens they issue from being transferred out of those wallets. That ability gives stablecoin companies an unusual role in crypto investigations: they can effectively immobilize stolen money before investigators identify the people responsible.

Tether froze more than 1.567B USDT related to a potential Binance exchange hack.

Protecting the industry and our partners is our main priority.

— Paolo Ardoino 🤖 (@paoloardoino) October 29, 2026

This is not the first time the Secret Service has worked alongside crypto companies to investigate digital asset crimes. In April, Operation Atlantic—a joint effort involving the Secret Service, the U.K.'s National Crime Agency, Binance, Coinbase, and other crypto firms— identified more than 20,000 victims of approval phishing scams, traced $45 million in stolen funds and froze $12 million.

The Binance investigation also comes against the backdrop of a growing number of attacks attributed to North Korea. Blockchain intelligence firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis have both linked the $6.3 billion theft to TraderTraitor, a hacking group associated with the country's cyber operations. The same group has been linked to the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025, which the FBI also attributed to North Korean hackers.

North Korean attackers have targeted Binance itself before, including through attempts to infiltrate the exchange by posing as job applicants. In an interview with Decrypt in 2025, Binance Chief Security Officer Jimmy Su said North Korean operatives were contacting employees on LinkedIn every day, sometimes using fake interviews to deliver malware to their devices.

Binance has said it will cover all losses using company funds and its Secure Asset Fund for Users, an emergency reserve designed to protect customers in the event of a security breach. CEO Richard Teng said on Friday that the company had reserved more than $6.34 billion to cover the stolen assets, while approximately $3 billion had already been located or frozen with the help of industry partners.

The exchange initially suspected that a compromised laptop belonging to a DevOps engineer had provided the attackers with access to its systems. It has since identified additional points of breach and said it found no evidence that wallet private keys had been leaked. Withdrawals remain suspended while Binance reviews its security infrastructure, with the company targeting a resumption by Sunday.

In a recent X discussion, Ripple CTO emeritus David Schwartz has clarified his current position at the company.

An X user had asked Schwartz whether he still serves as a non-executive director. "Are you not still a Non-Executive Director at Ripple?" the X user asked.

The question came after Schwartz said he no longer works for Ripple and had retired. "I don't work for Ripple anymore. I retired," Schwartz stated.In response to the X user's question, Schwartz said he remains on Ripple's Board of Directors and also serves as CTO Emeritus: "I'm on the Board of Directors and I'm CTO Emeritus."

@Lebro589 @CocaColaKid_OG @Fernando031680 @jtraulf1 @SergioVengeance @MAGASouthEastFL @Emmaiid11 I'm on the Board of Directors and I'm CTO Emeritus. I still meet with my former team twice a month and show up randomly at Ripple offices and say "hi".

Schwartz also said he still meets with his former team twice a month, while he occasionally visits Ripple's offices without a formal schedule, describing these visits as opportunities to say hello to his former colleagues: "I still meet with my former team twice a month and show up randomly at Ripple offices and say hi."

David Schwartz, one of the original architects of the XRP Ledger, became the Chief Technology Officer (CTO) of Ripple in July 2018, after previously serving as the company's chief cryptographer since 2011. He transitioned out of his day-to-day operating CTO role at the end of December 2025 to become CTO Emeritus and join Ripple's Board of Directors.

Despite stepping away from his full-time responsibilities, Schwartz indicated that he maintains a connection with the company and his former team.

Schwartz maintains he was not going away from the XRP community, participating in the XRP Ledger through his XRP node, which has now been running for several months, publishing its output data and researching other use cases for XRP besides what Ripple is focused on, among other things.

Shortly after Evernorth, a Ripple-backed XRP treasury firm, was revealed, Schwartz stated he would serve as a strategic advisor to the company, bringing his wealth of experience despite his retirement from Ripple.

At the Swell event, which was held at the end of October, Schwartz joined Ayo Akinyele, Head of Engineering for RippleX, in a conversation on what comes next for XRPL. The discussion explored the technologies shaping the ledger's evolution, the trade-offs behind them, and what it will take to support the next generation of financial and on-chain applications.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

StakerGPT trades crypto markets around the clock and publishes every trade it makes, as more than 8,200 users join the platform

StakerX.ai, an agentic trading platform built by Switzerland-based StakerX Strategies AG, has passed $40 million in assets staked on Solana less than three months after its public launch on July 13, 2026. Users fund their stakes with more than 40 cryptocurrencies from most major blockchains, and StakerGPT, the platform’s agentic trading agent, puts that capital to work in crypto perpetual futures markets every day.

An agentic system is AI software that carries out a multi-step task on its own. StakerGPT is built on rare-access Claude Mythos: it monitors crypto markets around the clock, reasons over potential trade setups, filters them through risk controls, and routes the approved trades for execution, without a human placing each order.

Every trade is public. StakerX publishes the full record daily at stakerx.ai/trades, with the market, direction, entry and exit prices, and return of each position, including both live-executed and signal-derived trades. As of October 18, 2026, the record holds 804 trades across 139 trading days since June 1, 2026, so anyone can check what the agent did on any given day instead of relying on summary figures.

Users can deposit USDC, USDT, BTC, ETH, SOL, BNB, TRX, and more than 30 other cryptocurrencies from Solana, Ethereum, Bitcoin, BNB Chain, Base, Arbitrum, Polygon, Tron, and other networks. Each deposit is converted and credited as USDC, a dollar-backed stablecoin, on Solana, so users do not need to bridge or swap anything themselves. Stakes start at $10 and run for 30 days.

As of October 18, 2026, StakerX.ai reports:

StakerX is preparing native mobile apps for iOS and Android, expected in December 2026, which will bring the dashboard, deposits, and the live StakerGPT trade record to the Apple App Store and Google Play. The platform is available today on the web at stakerx.ai.

StakerX is an agentic trading platform built and operated by StakerX Strategies AG, a quantitative trading and investment firm based in Switzerland. Its AI trading agent, StakerGPT, is built on rare-access Claude Mythos and evaluates crypto markets, applies risk filters, and routes trades, and the platform publishes every trade in its daily record. Its goal is to make agentic trading accessible to anyone who holds crypto, from any chain, with every trade on the record. StakerX launched publicly on July 13, 2026. More information is available at stakerx.ai.

#5137. No Ethereum (ETH)? Exchanges Report Substantial Outflows

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ethereum’s exchange supply is shrinking after the October 29 Binance hack, but on-chain data suggests there is something more interesting hiding behind the scenes. The two-stage decline in exchange-held ETH could reflect the liquidity contraction rather than a minor panic-fueled outflow.

Between October 28 and November 2, the share of Ethereum’s total supply held on exchanges dropped from 3.56% to 3.20%, a decline of 0.36%. Just over half of that drop happened on October 29, the same day as the reported Binance exploit.

According to TRM Labs, hackers drained around 200,630 ETH from Binance’s hot wallet.

Instead of dropping right away, ETH balances on top exchange addresses actually grew by around 39,000 ETH on October 29. The bigger drop came later. Those addresses lost roughly 313,000 ETH on November 1 and November 2, after withdrawals were reopened.

Major exchange addresses lost around 297,000 ETH, or 5.4%, between October 28 and November 2, suggesting a substantial liquidity thinning.

Falling exchange balances aren't necessarily suggesting an accumulation. The coins may have gone into cold storage, moved between wallets, or sent to other exchanges. Exchange-address data alone can’t show where they've been sent.

As of now, Ethereum’s price stays bearish. On the daily ETH/USD chart, ETH is trading near $2,264, below several key moving averages. The price has pulled back from its October highs around $2,800.

Selling pressure can be clearly seen on the chart, and the $2,300-$2,400 range could act as resistance. A proper recovery above the level would suggest an improvement in sentiment. But if buyers fail to defend the $2,200 area, Ethereum could drop even further.

For now, the current supply drop is more of a liquidity shift, rather than a confirmed supply shock, especially on gigantic exchanges like Binance.

The more important question is whether the missing ETH has actually left trading circulation or just moved elsewhere. Until market flows calm down a little, it’s too early to call the exchange supply drop a bullish signal.

#5136. Trump Iran Strikes Push Bitcoin Under $77K as Oil Jumps

Home » Crypto Market News » Trump Iran Strikes Push Bitcoin Under $77K as Oil Jumps

Surging oil and cascading liquidations send Bitcoin below short-term holder cost basis.

The crypto market faced renewed selling pressure on Tuesday, November 3, as geopolitical tensions flared again in the Middle East. Total crypto market capitalization fell 1.7% following reports of military actions on Iran’s Qeshm Island, reviving fears of a prolonged conflict and pushing oil higher.

The broader market impact of the Donald Trump Iran strikes pushed crude oil prices up by more than 1.6%, with Brent crude above $106.95 a barrel, as hopes for an immediate diplomatic resolution quickly faded.

As energy supply fears triggered a classic “risk-off” environment across global financial assets, Bitcoin led the crypto slide.

Bitcoin price fell 1.9% over 24 hours to trade near $76,950, extending its 7-day losses to 4%. Altcoins felt equal pain, with Ethereum (ETH) slipping 1.2% to $2,265 and Solana (SOL) declining 2.5% to $92.25.

The sharp move caught overly bullish derivatives traders off guard. According to CoinGlass, $382.48 million in leveraged positions were liquidated across crypto exchanges within 24 hours.

Long positions accounted for $341.81 million of the total wipeout, with Bitcoin traders absorbing nearly $100 million in forced closures.

The recent pullback highlights underlying fragility within the crypto market. A report from CryptoQuant noted that Bitcoin’s rebound faded after reaching $78,700 on Monday, when hopes for US-Iran talks and Binance restarting withdrawals after the historic hack supported sentiment. The rally stalled below $79,000, and a later recovery attempt failed as the Nasdaq closed down 1.1%.

BTC has also slipped below the $77,140 cost basis of 1–3 month holders, which raises the risk of selling into rebounds. CryptoQuant’s Apparent Demand metric has dropped into negative territory, below its 30-day average.

CryptoQuant’s report said some Bitcoin spot buying persists but has not absorbed futures selling and liquidations. However, as U.S. midterm elections take place and Trump Iran strikes continue, the macroeconomic backdrop remains uncertain and could fuel market volatility.Key

CryptoQuant analysts see $76,600–$76,700 as key support. Bitcoin needs to reclaim $77,100–$77,200, then $77,400–$77,600 for sustained recovery. Falling liquidations and steady spot buying would help support a recovery.

However, as US midterm elections take place and Trump strikes on Iran continue, economic uncertainty remains high, leaving markets vulnerable to further volatility.

#5135. $8 Million Crypto Kidnapping Lands Texas Brothers 15-Year Prison Terms

$8 Million Crypto Kidnapping Lands Texas Brothers 15-Year Prison Terms

Two Texas brothers have been sentenced to a combined 326 months in prison for a violent home-invasion robbery in which they held a Minnesota family at gunpoint for hours and forced the father to hand over more than $8 million in cryptocurrency.

Raymond Christian Garcia and Isiah Angelo Garcia, both of Waller, Texas, were sentenced on Thursday to 180 months and 186 months respectively, or 15 and 15-and-a-half years, by U.S. District Judge Donovan Frank in St. Paul, according to the Justice Department. Both had pleaded guilty in June.

According to court documents, the brothers traveled from Texas to Grant, Minnesota, and on Sept. 19, 2025, broke into the victim’s home, zip-tied the family and held them at gunpoint for more than nine hours while demanding access to the father’s crypto accounts.

When he failed to transfer the amount they expected, they forced him to drive one of the brothers to the family’s cabin in upstate Minnesota to retrieve additional hardware wallets and move the funds. In total, they compelled him to transfer over $8 million. The pair fled after the victim’s son called 911, and were later arrested near Houston.

“The Garcia brothers committed a brazen and calculated act of violence against the victim and his family,” U.S. Attorney Daniel Rosen said in a statement. The FBI’s Christopher Dotson said the brothers “violated the sanctity of a home” in “a brazen act of violence and greed.”

The case is a stark example of a “wrench attack,” in which criminals use physical force rather than hacking to seize crypto.

Such attacks have surged alongside crypto’s rising prices: Chainalysis counted 46 violent incidents in the first half of 2026 with more than $30 million stolen, while CertiK pegged losses even higher. Recent cases span a French kidnapping in which workers’ children were held hostage and a UK home invasion that ended in prison terms for the attackers.

The sentencing offers one of the clearest signals yet that U.S. courts are treating crypto wrench attacks as serious violent crime.

#5134. Kinetic’s $24 million XRP-linked lending market showed a 0.04% base rate before rewards

Kinetic’s November 1 snapshots separate modest FXRP loan demand from collateral use and incentive-supported returns.

Kinetic’s FXRP lending market on Flare showed roughly $24 million supplied on Nov. 1, yet the base annualized rate for lenders was about 0.04% before rewards. For XRP holders, the supplied balance alone is a weak measure of borrower-funded income.

Kinetic is a lending protocol on Flare, where FXRP represents XRP-linked exposure through the FAssets system. Its official market page displayed $24 million of FXRP supplied and $2 million borrowed in the Nov. 1 observation.

The paired stablecoin market told a different story. FlareSignal’s Nov. 1 lending snapshot put FXRP utilization at 6.7%, against 92.8% for USDT0. The gap helps explain why demand to borrow a token, demand to borrow against it, and the income paid to its holders require separate measures.

The FXRP supply APR in that snapshot was about 0.04%, while the FXRP borrower APR was about 0.7%. The supply figure describes a simple annualized base rate at the observation time.

Rewards change the displayed return. In the Nov. 1 capture, Kinetic’s official FXRP row showed a 0.59% supply APY and a separately displayed 0.55% rFLR reward component. The reward component belongs inside the quoted yield comparison; adding it again to the headline supply APY would overstate the display.

DefiLlama’s specific isoFXRP pool page separately labeled its 0.57% supply APY as a 30-day average, alongside a 0.04% base component and a +0.54% reward component labeled WFLR. Rounding and differing observation windows limit direct reconciliation with the app’s figures.

DefiLlama’s Kinetic adapter calculates base yield from supply rates and rewards from separate emissions and token prices. That separation identifies the economic question behind the headline: how much comes from borrowers paying for credit, and how much comes from incentive distributions? The observed reward component was much larger than the quoted base rate.

FlareSignal reported about 19.45 million FXRP supplied and 1.31 million FXRP borrowed in the dated observation. It also reported about 5.04 million USDT0 supplied and 4.68 million USDT0 borrowed. The utilization figures show substantially different demand for borrowing the two assets within the isolated market.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Supplied FXRP may also support borrowing when enabled as collateral and permitted by market rules. Kinetic’s lending documentation describes suppliers enabling assets as collateral to borrow other assets, with borrowers required to remain overcollateralized. Someone seeking stablecoin liquidity may value an XRP-linked collateral position while having little use for an additional FXRP loan.

Collateral utility gives the holder access to borrowing. Any cash return to a supplier remains a separate benefit. A holder may value access to credit while receiving little base interest on the supplied asset.

The USDT0 figure reports aggregate market debt. Kinetic’s isolated-market contract directory identifies the market’s assets and infrastructure, but attributing that debt to FXRP collateral requires individual account data.

XRP is the XRP Ledger’s native asset, while the asset supplied to this Kinetic market is FXRP on Flare. That distinction places additional protocol steps between holding native XRP and having a lending position.

Flare’s XRP minting documentation describes sending underlying XRP to the Core Vault on the XRP Ledger, followed by an executor finalizing the FXRP mint on Flare using verified payment evidence. The FAssets system connects that representation to underlying assets and collateral arrangements.

The return journey has conditions too. Under the ordinary holder redemption process, FAssets are burned and underlying payment is initiated through the protocol. Fees and redemption-ticket limits can apply. If an agent fails to pay, compensation can involve collateral after proof of nonpayment.

Kinetic’s Nov. 1 observations show substantial FXRP supplied relative to FXRP loans, with a reward component much larger than base lender interest. For this lending market, the test for lasting demand is continued use of credit and the interest borrowers pay for it. The supplied balance establishes market size; those cash flows would establish a stronger foundation for lender income.

US spot Bitcoin ETFs saw $398.62 million in net daily outflows, led by Fidelity’s FBTC ($164 million), while 44,857 ETH left spot Ethereum ETFs.

Binance posted a record $15.4 billion weekly net outflow following a $6.3 billion hack the FBI attributed to North Korea.

This week’s key US triggers are the midterm elections and JOLTS (Tuesday), ISM services (Wednesday), the Fed balance sheet (Thursday) and Nonfarm Payrolls (Friday).

The crypto market greeted Tuesday morning, November 3, with a massive wave of margin position closures. Over the past 24 hours, the positions of 94,997 traders were forcibly liquidated for a total of $386.42 million, with 88% of all losses ($339.92 million) falling on long contracts, while Bitcoin ($100.74 million) and Ethereum ($49.31 million) led in liquidation volume as per CoinGlass.

A strong bearish skew persists in the US spot ETF sector: the net daily outflow from BTC funds amounted to $398.62 million, with the main blow falling on the Fidelity fund (FBTC), which saw $164 million leave. Over the day, 44,857 ETH were withdrawn from spot Ethereum ETFs. The combined assets under management (AUM) of all crypto ETFs stand at $95.83 billion as per SoSoValue.

The current daily outflow has spread across the entire line of exchange-traded products: SOL funds lost 129.19 thousand coins, outflows from XRP products amounted to 128.54 thousand tokens, and the specialized HYPE and ZEC funds shed 85.07 thousand and 13.53 thousand coins, respectively. Major operators are preemptively pulling liquidity out of volatile instruments ahead of the US midterm elections.

Amid the decline in market capitalization, a sharp split among participants has emerged: while retail investors are locking in losses en masse and most whales are betting on a decline, some large on-chain operators are opening aggressive counter-trend positions on decentralized exchanges.

The main event of the morning session was the actions of an unknown major player on the Hyperliquid platform, who, despite the overall market decline (NEAR dropped to $3.79, BNB to $697.67), stubbornly holds an XRP long worth tens of millions of dollars, turning the contract into the main barometer of local altcoin resilience.

The decentralized futures platform Hyperliquid is currently accumulating peak institutional volumes. At the same time, on-chain analytics of top wallets with balances of $1 million or more shows a strong bearish skew in open interest.

Of these wallets' total open positions of $4.5 billion, short contracts (shorts) account for $3.08 billion. Their long volume is limited to a modest $1.46 billion.

Leviathan addresses with positions of $50 million or more are almost unanimously betting on a further market decline. Against this grim backdrop, the long position of wallet '0x31b1ecf848443472e34d204a134812d86ab01434' remains the largest and riskiest counter-trend position in the system.

The whale holds 35.03 million XRP tokens. The notional value of the open position is $44.05 million at 10x Cross leverage.

The average entry price is $1.1298. At the peak of the morning momentum, the whale's unrealized profit exceeded $4.47 million, which amounts to more than 101% net return on the margin capital invested.

At the same time, the account's overall result is far from ideal: due to overall asset volatility, this wallet's net PnL over the past 30 days has fallen by $8.45 million.

The critical threshold in this whole story remains the forced liquidation price. The automatic position closure threshold sits at $0.9626.

The current XRP market price of around $1.2558 leaves the trader a buffer of about 23% to the liquidation point.

Large wallets' XRP positions on Hyperliquid are also skewed toward the bears. Against long positions of $55.70 million stands a massive wall of shorts worth $80.93 million. Nearly 80% of these longs belong to a single whale.

This morning's news backdrop points to a massive and painful restructuring of the entire market infrastructure. The central theme remains a severe liquidity crisis on centralized exchanges.

$6.3 billion Binance hack and the FBI's position: The agency officially attributed the attack to North Korean state cyber units. An analysis by lawyers at Reed Smith and Lathrop GPM showed that the exchange's insurance policies will not cover the damage. Standard D&O insurance protects only top management, while war exclusion clauses fully rule out payouts in the event of cyberwarfare waged by foreign states.

Record outflow of $15.4 billion: The weekly net capital outflow from Binance hit an all-time high. This volume exceeds the individual reserves of Bybit or Bitfinex and surpasses the combined balance of Gate, Bitget and KuCoin, which keeps the price of BNB at local lows.

Binance founder Changpeng Zhao's loud statements about rising risks to the financial system from automated AI-powered cyberattacks came amid the incident at the exchange he created, which showed the ineffectiveness of classic defense mechanisms against coordinated cyber operations. Zhao stressed that commercial banks, due to outdated IT architecture, are more vulnerable than crypto platforms.

Against this backdrop, decentralized protocols are confidently expanding their institutional presence. Hyperliquid's annual revenue has reached $713 million, and trading volume in perpetuals on stocks and commodities via HIP-3 has exceeded $500 billion. Building on these technologies, the operator of the New York Stock Exchange (ICE) and Kraken are already preparing to launch regulated futures in the US with White House support.

Meanwhile, serious changes are also taking place at the level of the largest L1 ecosystems. House of Stake (NEAR Protocol) participants approved the deflationary initiative HSP-029 with 99.96% of the vote, reducing annual emissions from 2.5% to 1.6% over 24 months. Daily issuance of new tokens is being cut from 89,500 to 57,300, while amid the general market decline the asset briefly dropped to $3.79.

Skepticism is meanwhile growing in the Ripple camp: the company reports that issuance of its dollar token RLUSD has surpassed $1 billion through partnerships with trust banks, but XRP holders are voicing open dissatisfaction. RLUSD is a centralized asset subject to freezing under US law, while XRP's decentralized status is not yet reflected in its market valuation.

At the same time, the Ethereum Foundation launched a platform for using the network as digital public infrastructure (DPI) for governments. Case studies on digital identity in Bhutan and confidential humanitarian aid systems have been published. At Devcon 8 in Mumbai, Vitalik Buterin called for developing verifiable code to minimize dependence on closed software and AI systems.

In the coming sessions, capital flows in the crypto market will be largely determined by external macroeconomic and political triggers. Investors and market makers need to keep the following critical factors in focus:

US Congressional midterm elections: The main political factor of this Tuesday, forcing large funds to preemptively close margin positions, cut leverage and hedge risks ahead of a change in the regulatory landscape.

Official JOLTS labor market report: Tuesday's release of job openings data will determine the short-term dynamics of the US dollar index and directly affect liquidity in high-risk assets.

ISM non-manufacturing business activity indices: Wednesday's statistics will highlight the real state of the US economy and shape the medium-term sentiment of major futures market operators.

Fed balance sheet on Thursday and Friday's Nonfarm Payrolls report: The key macroeconomic triggers of the week, which will lock in the final direction of capital flows through the end of the month.

#5132. 131 Million ADA Delegated to Dead Cardano Pools, Not Earning

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

According to popular Cardano explorer Cexplorer there are over 131 million ADA currently delegated to retired stake pools, leaving thousands of delegators without staking rewards.

Cardano staking pools offer ADA holders a way to passively earn rewards for securing the blockchain by delegating their coins to network validators, all without locking up or losing control of their funds.

Cexplorer recently posted on X that 59,025 delegators still have their ADA delegated to pools that have retired. Therefore, they are not earning staking rewards on their current delegations.

⚠️ Over 131M $ADA is currently delegated to retired stake pools. 59,025 delegators staking with those pools are not earning any staking rewards. If you're one of them, consider choosing a new stake pool to keep earning rewards and supporting Cardano's decentralization. https://t.co/L9mitriW2P

According to Cexplorer data, 131.63 million ADA is still delegated to retired pools and the number of retired pools is given as 2,720. Holders can delegate ADA to a stake pool and earn rewards as long as the pool they choose is active, which contributes to network security and decentralization.

However, when a stake pool retires, delegators must select another active pool if they want to continue earning rewards. Their ADA does not disappear simply because the pool has retired, but the delegation no longer provides the expected staking benefits.

Users who are delegating their ADA to such retired pools are urged to review their staking arrangements and consider moving their ADA to active stake pools.

Major crypto exchange Binance has commenced phased withdrawals for crypto networks days after it suffered a hack that impacted nearly $6.3 billion in client assets. In line with this, Binance will reopen withdrawals for Cardano (ADA) on the BSC network beginning at 04:00 (UTC) on November 3, 2026.

In Dijkstra upgrade news, Node 11.2 is out as a pre-release. It carries the full Dijkstra hard fork feature set apart from Leios and will move DijkstraNet into the Dijkstra era so downstream teams can start integration testing.

Node 11.3 includes Leios and is set to deliver the complete Dijkstra feature set. A tagged pre-release is realistically three to four weeks away, two at the earliest.

#5131. Binance Hack: Why Bitcoin Didn't Crash Like It Did in 2018

Binance Hack: Why Bitcoin Didn't Crash Like It Did in 2018

Bitcoin is down about 4% a day after the biggest crypto hack on record. In March 2018, a failed attack on the same exchange knocked nearly 11% off its price in minutes, and Binance said at the time that no funds had been stolen.

Binance has confirmed yesterday's attack, and Chainalysis and TRM Labs put the theft at roughly $6.4 billion, more than four times the previous record hack: the $1.5 billion Bybit heist of February 2025. Bitcoin dipped to about $76,400 on Friday, around 6% below where it traded after Wednesday’s Federal Reserve decision.

More than $1.3 billion in leveraged bets was liquidated, meaning trades placed with borrowed money that exchanges close automatically when prices move against them. And yet Bitcoin is holding relatively steady, all things considered.

The 2018 incident was a phishing and theft attempt: attackers took over accounts, created API keys (credentials that let trading bots act on an account) and used them to pump the obscure coin Viacoin while 31 accounts that had pre-deposited the coin sold it. Bitcoin fell from $10,740 to $9,690 within minutes and kept dropping to about $6,600 by April 5, though the market was already in a bear run after peaking near $20,000 in December 2017.

To have a grasp of the situation, the entire crypto market was worth just over $400 billion in March 2018 and is worth roughly $2.6 trillion now.

A big reason for that, and why panic doesn't hit markets the same way anymore, is U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs. Funds like BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust let people buy Bitcoin through an ordinary brokerage account, and they now hold roughly 1.3 million BTC, about 6% of the supply.

Their shareholders don’t keep coins on Binance, and sovereign wealth funds such as Mubadala and the Abu Dhabi Investment Council have been buying. BlackRock’s fund alone took in $136 million on Wednesday, per Farside Investors data.

These funds don’t tend to panic sell so easily, and those funds remain locked for long periods of time.

The theft began at 4:05 p.m. ET Thursday, after U.S. markets closed, so Thursday’s preliminary $17 million of ETF inflows predate it and Friday’s flow figures are the first real read on whether those holders are selling.

The attacker still holds more than 420,000 ETH and 18,270 BTC, about $2.4 billion that no stablecoin issuer can freeze. The amount doesn’t account for other tokens, which would increase the holdings to more than $3 billion.

TRM and Chainalysis tie the attack to the North Korean group behind Bybit, which after that heist converted about 84% of the stolen Ethereum into Bitcoin through Thorchain, a cross-chain swap network.

Binance says it has set aside more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover every loss, and it is buying the missing tokens on the open market and over the counter, meaning in private deals off the exchange. About $3 billion is frozen, deposits are operating normally, and withdrawals are on track to resume in 45 hours.

Market sentiment was already soft before the theft and slid further: CoinMarketCap’s Fear and Greed index, a 0-to-100 gauge of market mood, dropped to 36 points from 43 (now in “fear” territory after being “neutral” yesterday), and Bitcoin sits about 38% below its $126,080 record. Back in 2018, the market was well in “extreme fear” territory at 12 points on the scale when the incident happened. Traders were already in panic mode, and the attack just worsened an already ugly situation.

Glassnode data show long-term holders underwater on about 4 million coins bought between $85,000 and $118,000, and HashKey analyst Tim Sun said before the hack that the market’s problem “is not panic selling, but rather insufficient new buying interest.”

So, taken all together, what it all boils down to is this: A more optimistic market, much more liquidity, active long-term investors, a clearer regulatory framework, a sense of safety thanks to that regulatory clarity, quick action by stablecoin providers, and a price trend in recovery likely all played a role in keeping Bitcoin safe from a global panic despite the record-breaking hack.

#5130. Ethena’s trading team can approve borrowers of assets backing USDe under new rules

The October 29 framework delegates qualifying borrower screening while retaining committee call-in powers and oversight of lending limits.

Ethena’s October 29 institutional-lending framework sets out a route for its trading team to onboard qualifying borrowers of assets backing USDe without an individual Risk Committee vote.

The posted rules describe a change in approval authority over lending that helps back Ethena’s synthetic dollar. For USDe holders, published eligibility rules and exposure disclosures offer a way to assess that credit risk. The committee retains oversight, with borrower documents sent to it privately.

The framework posted by Kairos Research describes the delegated route; it identifies no newly funded borrower using it.

Eligibility combines bilateral lending with full recourse to the borrower or a parent guarantee, jurisdiction and standing checks, sanctions screening, segregated tri-party custody and verifiable daily collateral reports. The custody arrangement puts collateral with a third party.

Borrower documents go privately to the committee at least three business days before first funding. A member calling a borrower in must identify a failed criterion, with a decision within five business days. The committee therefore retains a criterion-based call-in power, while the text leaves funding during a pending objection unspecified. Cases outside the framework still require committee review.

The lending team sets loan-to-value rate cards, shared monthly with the committee alongside book reporting. Loan-to-value measures the amount lent relative to collateral value. A missed margin call, default or changed standing stops new lending to that counterparty and triggers escalation within 48 hours.

The permitted collateral includes BTC, ETH and other specified assets. USDe, sUSDe, ENA, USDtb and borrowers’ own assets are excluded; Ethereum-based XAUT is conditional on custody, liquidation venues and a weekend liquidation test.

The framework preserves five existing approvals. Its public text sets no numerical aggregate lending cap or per-borrower concentration ceiling.

Ethena’s institutional-lending documentation places aggregate exposure limits under governance and committee review, without publishing a numerical ceiling.

The earlier partner-by-partner approach is visible in the March 10 Maple and Anchorage proposal. It said additional offchain partners would need new proposals to the Risk Committee. LlamaRisk, Kairos Research and Blockworks Advisory subsequently posted support for the reviewed agreements.

Those reviews already kept contractual terms confidential. The October development concerns approval authority; confidentiality predates it.

The March discussion also shows why a partner’s brand is insufficient to identify the legal claim. Anchorage’s agreement was with A1, Ltd., a Cayman Islands group entity distinct from the OCC-chartered Anchorage Digital Bank. Maple’s pool-based agreement ran through Delaware’s Protocol Pool Operations Alpha LLC.

LlamaRisk’s April legal-review methodology names Ethena (BVI) Limited as the entity deploying reserve-backing assets. It examines collateral ratios, custody segregation, perfected security interests, liquidation rights, recall and redelivery timing, and practical enforceability.

The signing entity and contract matter because they govern the claim on borrowed assets and collateral.

Retaining the five existing approvals preserves arrangements with different structures; it does not establish that every legacy pool or warehouse meets the new bilateral criteria. LlamaRisk’s August 4 FalconX review described warehouse financing to FalconX International Lending Opportunities SPC for its segregated portfolio SP 1. The vehicle acquires institutional loan receivables and pledges its assets to Ethena, rather than operating as an ordinary bilateral borrower.

FalconX then announced a $1 billion secured SPV facility on August 19. That was an announced financing capacity, not a verified outstanding loan balance on November 1.

LlamaRisk’s October 12 assessment allocated 14.3% of backing to institutional lending, against total reported backing of roughly $5 billion and a similar USDe supply.

Blockworks Advisory’s Q3 governance update, published October 13, put institutional lending at 8% of liquid cash as of September 30. The two percentages use different dates and denominators. Comparing them as a rise from 8% to 14.3% would therefore be misleading.

CryptoSlate’s earlier lending analysis examined how allocations and recovery terms affect redemption liquidity. The October framework adds the question of which borrowers may enter through delegated screening.

Ethena’s lending dashboard describes collateral held with custodians, continuous LTV monitoring and no rehypothecation, meaning collateral is not pledged onward. In the November 1 text capture available for this report, borrower rows and the update time were unpopulated. It therefore provides no current borrower-level exposure or collateral ratios.

For holders, a usable assessment would put those elements together: the signing entity, its current outstanding amount, collateral and LTV, concentration against the relevant limit, and information to assess enforceability and recovery prospects.

#5129. Mercado Bitcoin wallets show post-hack transfers after $46M theft

Brazil’s exchange Mercado Bitcoin paused deposits and withdrawals after a security breach, saying customer funds and data were safe.

Mercado Bitcoin, one of Brazil’s largest cryptocurrency exchanges, confirmed a security incident on Monday that blockchain analysts estimated drained more than $46 million from its wallets. Despite the company’s announcement that it had suspended deposits and withdrawals, addresses associated with the incident continued to receive funds for hours afterward.

Senior security engineer at Certik, Dickson Wu, published an initial post-mortem of the incident, saying the attackers “picked at eight small chains for four hours,” including Base, Optimism and Arbitrum. The attack then moved to the company’s main hot wallets before transferring tiny amounts of Ether (ETH) from 27,160 different addresses.

“So whoever did this can also move money out of MB’s customer deposit addresses,” he wrote.

Security firms have stated the hack was carried out through standard transfers rather than a smart contract exploit. Mercado Bitcoin said the breach would have no financial impact on customers.

According to Wu, hours after Mercado Bitcoin announced a temporary suspension of deposits and withdrawals, funds continued to move out of addresses tied to the breach, followed by thousands of small ETH transfers into a single address.

A spokesperson for Mercado Bitcoin told Cointelegraph that customers had suffered no losses and no data had been leaked, but the company did not respond to requests for comment on the continued transfers. A data breach could also trigger notification requirements to Brazil’s data protection authority.

The hack has not yet been attributed to an organized group and remains under investigation.

Of the stolen funds, by 4:42 pm UTC approximately $6.2 million had been frozen by stablecoin issuers Tether and Circle. About $20 million had been transferred into newly created wallets, while over $20 million remained in associated addresses identified by Mercado Bitcoin, including all 106 BTC.

Mercado Bitcoin had 4.5 million users as of July, when stablecoin issuer Tether announced a $20 million investment to support its expansion in tokenization, payments and credit. Another Tether-backed exchange in Latin America, Chile’s Orionx, announced its closure in September after a forensic audit found that more than $7 million in customer assets had moved to wallets outside its control.

The incident comes days after Binance reported a $6.3 billion hack targeting its hot wallets on Thursday. The exchange suspended deposits and withdrawals and pledged to cover customer losses. Binance began resuming withdrawals on Sunday, saying it had replenished 95% of the stolen assets.

#5128. Aave Horizon taps perpetual futures to price stock collateral on weekends, Chainlink says

The moving reference can reveal off-hours collateral declines without ensuring a buyer for seized assets.

Chainlink says Aave's institutional lending platform Horizon is using its new weekend pricing service, giving tokenized stock collateral a reference that keeps moving while traditional exchanges are closed. The service was announced Oct. 29 and starts with high-volume U.S. equities, according to Chainlink's announcement.

The off-hours price comes from derivatives markets. During regular, extended and overnight sessions, Chainlink 24/7 uses its existing weekday data. When the underlying market closes, it draws quotes from multiple perpetual-futures venues, where derivatives linked to the asset continue trading. That reference reflects those markets' prices, rather than transactions on a closed stock exchange.

Chainlink says it filters stale inputs, limits outlier influence and joins the different sessions into one continuous series. Lending protocols receive the prices through onchain Data Feeds.

For a borrower, the consequence depends on how the feed enters collateral calculations. Where the moving reference is integrated into the market's risk rules, a weekend decline can reduce the value supporting a loan before the underlying exchange reopens. A position could cross its liquidation threshold during that window, rather than waiting for the reopening price update.

Horizon is a separate institutional instance of Aave. Only participants qualified and allowlisted by the collateral's issuer can supply its tokenized real-world assets and borrow against them. Its stablecoin pools, including USDC, RLUSD and GHO, accept liquidity from unrestricted suppliers. Collateral requirements and liquidation thresholds depend on the underlying asset.

For those stablecoin lenders, a moving valuation can make deteriorating collateral visible sooner. Debt recovery still depends on what can be realized from the collateral; an oracle reference by itself supplies neither a buyer nor redemption access.

Chainlink's announcement and Aave's Horizon documentation do not disclose the initial equity tokens, their feed addresses or equity-specific liquidation settings. Horizon's documentation describes bounds on issuer-reported net asset values that reject out-of-range updates and retain the last valid price. How those safeguards apply to the newly announced equity feeds is not specified in those pages.

This is distinct from Aave's V4 Equities Hub on Base, the subject of CryptoSlate's September weekend-gap coverage. Aave's Sept. 25 description of that market listed seven Coinbase stock tokens and feeds that held their last value over weekends. The Horizon announcement does not establish that Base's configuration has changed.

#5127. Reddit Stock Plunges 30% as Google's AI Search Hits Traffic and User Growth

Reddit Stock Plunges 30% as Google's AI Search Hits Traffic and User Growth

Reddit shares plunged 30.44% to $90.15 on Friday after CEO Steve Huffman warned that Google's AI-powered search features are cutting referral traffic and could shrink the platform's user base.

The selloff came after the company reported strong third-quarter results but issued a weaker-than-expected revenue forecast for the current quarter.

Reddit built much of its business around people discovering its discussions through Google searches, and those visitors are increasingly finding answers without ever clicking through to the platform. That puts pressure on the advertising business that has helped turn Reddit into one of the internet's fastest-growing publicly traded companies.

Reddit's third-quarter numbers initially looked healthy. Revenue climbed 53% year over year to $895 million, advertising revenue increased 54% to $847 million, and net income reached $283 million. Diluted earnings per share, which measure company profit attributable to each share while accounting for potential dilution, came in at $1.40, up 75% from a year earlier and above the $1.33 analyst consensus.

But the audience figures were less encouraging since Reddit reported 130.3 million daily active unique visitors, up 12% year over year but below the roughly 134 million analysts expected. In the U.S., the number actually fell 2% to 50.7 million, giving investors another reason to question whether the platform can maintain its growth.

Huffman delivered the warning during Thursday's earnings call, explaining that Google search referrals had fallen by more than one million on average every week during October, with the decline accelerating toward the end of the third quarter. If that pace continues, Reddit's total daily active users could fall by a low double-digit percentage in Q4 from the current base of around 130 million.

Huffman pointed directly at Google's AI Overviews and AI Mode, features that generate answers and summaries directly inside search results. Rather than sending users to websites to find information, Google increasingly gives them what they are looking for on its own page, pushing traditional search links further down.

Asked whether the changes were responsible for Reddit's declining search traffic, Huffman said the AI features had expanded aggressively in the U.S. and internationally. "The AI mode does not drive traffic, not for us and not to anybody as far as I know," he told investors.

That creates an awkward situation for Reddit—and arguably every other single media site on the internet. People can still search for Reddit discussions, but Google's results increasingly allow them to consume information from those discussions without visiting Reddit itself. Search referrals also bring back existing users, meaning fewer clicks can affect both new-user acquisition and the platform's ability to keep people engaged.

Reddit is trying to reduce that dependence by moving toward a primarily logged-in experience, which would require people to sign into an account before accessing most of the platform. The company plans to begin testing the change in selected markets outside the U.S. during the fourth quarter.

The strategy also connects to Reddit's increasingly important relationship with the AI industry. In February 2024, Reddit signed a reported $60 million-a-year data licensing deal with Google, giving the search giant access to Reddit discussions to train AI models. A few months later, the company announced a similar partnership with OpenAI, allowing the ChatGPT developer to use Reddit content while giving Reddit access to OpenAI's technology.

Those deals turned Reddit's enormous archive of human conversations into something it could sell to AI developers. But the same AI-powered search experience that benefits from Reddit's content can reduce the number of people who actually visit the site. Reddit also started tightening access to its public data and moving toward a more restricted platform which angered long term users.

The stock has now lost 44% over the past week, with Friday's 30.44% plunge accounting for a significant portion of the decline. At $90.15, Reddit is trading well below the levels it held just days ago.

The technical picture offers little immediate relief. Reddit's 50-day exponential moving average, a trend indicator that gives greater weight to recent prices, sits around $149.80, while its 200-day average is near $162.12. The 50-day average has already fallen below the 200-day average, creating a so-called death cross, a bearish signal commonly associated with a longer-term downward trend.

At $90.15, Reddit would need to climb approximately 66%—almost 70%—just to return to its 50-day average. That would only bring the share price back to the shorter-term trend line, not undo the death cross itself. With both averages sitting far above the current price, a recovery to those levels in the near term looks increasingly unlikely.

#5126. Solana study finds wider spreads outside aggregators at five of six sampled pools

The historical SOL/USDC comparison matched opposite-side fills within the same pools; wallet fees and current net execution costs are separate measures.

A September study found wider realized round-trip spreads for Solana trades routed outside aggregators at five of six major proprietary liquidity venues, suggesting that access to the same pool did not mean access to comparable trading terms.

The result puts the route into the center of the price comparison. A pool can settle trades on a public blockchain while a single operator controls its quotes. Comparing pool addresses alone therefore leaves out an important part of the transaction: how the order arrived.

The preprint by Ozan Solmaz, Lioba Heimbach and Jason Milionis, posted Sept. 29, examined proprietary automated market makers, or propAMMs, on Solana, Base and Monad. Its observations run from Sept. 1, 2025, through Aug. 31, 2026, with later starts for newer deployments. The Solana finding is a historical association between routing class and realized spreads, rather than a measurement of what every interface delivers today.

An aggregator searches across liquidity venues and can split an order among them. Jupiter is one such routing provider on Solana. A propAMM is the venue supplying liquidity: one operator provides inventory and can update prices directly, without waiting for a trade to change the pool's reserves.

The study compared aggregator and non-aggregator flow within the six largest measured Solana propAMMs for SOL/USDC. To estimate a realized round-trip spread, it paired consecutive fills on opposite sides of the same pool, within 25 slots and with trade sizes within a factor of two. There could be no intervening quote update from the operator, and both legs had to belong to the same routing class.

The researchers then compared the classes within cells defined by pool, day and trade-size bucket. That makes the test more informative than comparing unrelated venues or differently sized orders. It still pairs observed trades; the two fills need not be one customer's actual buy-and-sell journey.

Source: the study's Table 1. All spread figures are realized round-trip measurements. The matched premium is the median within-cell difference; the final column counts matched cells, not trades or trading volume.

One basis point is 0.01 percentage point. The distinction between the columns matters. Tessera's overall medians were 0.9 and 0.93 basis points, while its median matched premium was 0.1 basis points. The matched result comes from comparing the two classes inside each cell before summarizing those differences. Subtracting the displayed overall medians would answer a different question.

For the first five venues, non-aggregator spreads were wider in 68% to 83% of matched cells, with reported exact sign-test p-values below 0.001. ZeroFi went the other way on the matched median, with a small negative premium.

These are round-trip price gaps, not individual swap fees. Nor does the classification identify ordinary retail customers: non-aggregator flow can have different characteristics from aggregator flow. Matching pool, day and size reduces some differences between the groups, but it does not turn the comparison into a randomized test of what happens when the same person changes interfaces.

The economic rationale starts with stale prices. A passive pool can lose money when another trader acts on a market move before the pool's price catches up. PropAMM operators can send price updates without executing a swap, reducing that exposure when their updates arrive before incoming trades.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

That ability can benefit the operator through fewer stale-price losses. In the Solana sample, aggregator flow was also associated with tighter matched spreads at most of the measured venues.

The paper offers a more direct mechanism example on Base. In the Tessera implementation it decoded there, the researchers identified six fee components: base, age, priority, mode, default and address. The transaction sender and the contract calling Tessera's interface can affect default and address treatment, including exemptions and penalties.

That demonstrates how a proprietary venue can make caller identity part of execution pricing. It is evidence about the inspected Base implementation, rather than proof that Tessera's Solana deployment, or every other Solana propAMM, uses the same code or fee rules.

A separate issue is whether an attractive quote survives until settlement. The researchers documented Base Tessera fee decreases near block ends and increases near block starts, helping explain differences between the previous block's quote and the eventual fill. They explicitly lacked historical Solana quote data to corroborate an analogous Solana quote-to-fill gap.

Aggregator 0x's March investigation also reported Base quote-to-fill discrepancies involving Flashblock timing and spread changes. Its findings belong to that chain and to an interested routing provider's investigation.

A wallet charge can affect the amount a trader receives even when the underlying pool offers competitive terms. Phantom's current swap disclosure, checked Nov. 2, lists a 0.85% fee for most swaps, with exceptions, alongside variable network fees. The exceptions include specified Cash-account flows and deposits to a perps balance.

That wallet fee is a different measurement from the paper's round-trip spread. Price impact describes how an order's own size affects its price. Slippage describes the movement between a quote and execution. A slippage tolerance sets how much deterioration a transaction permits; raising it does not create a fixed fee, but it allows a worse fill.

Jupiter's current API documentation shows why the interface label also leaves questions unanswered. Its managed order path lets Metis, JupiterZ, Dflow and OKX compete, while its customizable build path uses Metis only. JupiterZ supplies request-for-quote liquidity, a separate mechanism from an on-chain propAMM pool.

Jupiter documents a 2-basis-point platform fee for SOL-to-stablecoin swaps on the managed order path, with other schedules for other categories. It lists no Jupiter swap fee on the build path, while integrator charges and network costs remain separate considerations.

Those disclosures describe available integrations and charges. They do not identify the route used by a particular Phantom swap or establish that a wallet avoids aggregators.

Adding a historical matched spread premium to a current wallet fee would therefore produce a misleading savings estimate. The comparison would mix a paired round-trip statistic with a charge on a particular swap, without establishing which costs were incremental or already reflected in the delivered amount.

Jupiter describes two approaches to reducing the quote-to-fill gap. Its execution-estimation account says routing incorporates observed execution data and simulation. Its JIT Swap description says candidate pools are carried into the transaction and re-quoted on-chain, allowing the selected pools or split to change when another candidate allocation offers a better output.

The useful test is therefore the completed trade: the route and venue used, the relevant charges, and the amount actually delivered under comparable conditions.

#5124. Worried About Crypto Hacks? Buy Bitcoin Proxies in Stock Market Instead, Say Wall Street Firms

Worried About Crypto Hacks? Buy Bitcoin Proxies in Stock Market Instead, Say Wall Street Firms

Wall Street analysts are telling clients the record-breaking hack of Binance is a buying opportunity, not a reason to flee, urging investors to scoop up crypto-linked stocks while prices are depressed.

Analysts at Cantor Fitzgerald, TD Cowen, and Compass Point said in Friday research notes that the roughly $6.3 billion theft, the largest crypto hack ever, is unlikely to derail the broader market’s recovery.

The reassurance came a day after the exploit rattled markets and sent Bitcoin sliding toward $77,000.

Cantor’s Ramsey El-Assal pointed to how fast the industry moved to contain the damage, noting that stablecoin issuers and others froze roughly $3 billion of the stolen funds, with Tether freezing more than $1.5 billion, Circle about $800 million and Ripple around $200 million.

El-Assal expects shares of Coinbase, Robinhood, and Strategy to recover once the panic fades. “We would be buyers on weakness,” he wrote, adding that while the episode is painful, “this is not 2022.”

TD Cowen’s Lance Vitanza struck a similar note, arguing the breach exposed weaknesses in Binance’s own security rather than in the underlying technology behind Bitcoin, and framing the selloff as a chance to add to Bitcoin and the stocks of public companies that hold it.

Compass Point’s Ed Engel and Michael Donovan said Binance has “more than enough liquidity to cover the hole,” while flagging that reimbursing customers could strain stablecoin supply and that rivals like Coinbase and Hyperliquid could pick up business. They are watching the $75,000 to $77,000 Bitcoin range as a key level.

The logic behind buying stocks over tokens is that regulated, exchange-listed proxies like Coinbase, Robinhood, and Strategy offer crypto exposure without the custody risk the hack laid bare, and tend to rebound once sentiment steadies.

Not everyone is sanguine. Cristiano Ventricelli, vice president of digital assets at Moody’s Ratings, said the incident “underscores the heightened cybersecurity risks associated with hot wallets” and “illustrates the risk concentration inherent in large, centralized intermediaries.”

He cautioned that the broader implications will hinge on “the ultimate losses, affected counterparties, asset recovery, and importantly, whether the incident leads to wider disruption or loss of confidence.”

Binance has set aside $6.34 billion to cover losses and told users their assets are safe, while the Secret Service and FBI have opened a probe and firms including TRM and Chainalysis have tied the hack to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group.

#5121. Dollar stablecoin use stayed elevated after capital controls ended, new research suggests

Reuter reports no detectable reversal after controls were removed; his historical estimates distinguish persistent transfers from dollar holdings and monetary-policy effects.

Dollar stablecoin activity can remain elevated after capital controls end, according to historical estimates presented by IMF economist Marco Reuter.

His Oct. 31 VoxEU column adds a persistence finding to the debate over stablecoin dollarization. Earlier Bank for International Settlements research points in a similar direction on the durability of dollar access. The evidence is less conclusive about how much monetary control governments lose: persistent transfers, lasting dollar holdings and a weaker response to interest rates are different outcomes.

Reuter compares 217 activations of capital-flow measures with countries that introduced no measure in the surrounding months. The estimates use the month before activation as their baseline. Following tighter restrictions on capital outflows, estimated cross-border stablecoin activity rises roughly 30% within six months and remains elevated through the twelfth month.

In a separate analysis of subsequent removals, he reports no detectable retreat. He also says the data reject the hypothesis that removals fully reverse the initial increase. The column does not give a precise post-removal duration.

The different responses to tightening and removal have a practical policy consequence. If activity simply tracked the presence of a restriction, easing it should bring activity back down. The reported pattern instead suggests that lifting controls alone may be insufficient to return stablecoin use to its earlier level.

The timing matters. The column was published in October 2026, but the underlying USDT and USDC panel covers 188 countries during 2018–2025.

Reuter measures activity using cross-border stablecoin inflows. That can reveal a larger market for dollar access without establishing that more wealth has left the country on a net basis. Gross receipts and net capital outflows answer different questions.

Country attribution also introduces uncertainty. Reuter assigns locations probabilistically using wallet activity and exchange website audiences.

Holdings are another distinct measure. Reuter approximates them through cumulative net flows, including transfers involving issuers; those estimates depend on inferred wallet locations and transaction coverage.

His separate currency-crisis comparison reports combined estimated holdings rising about $7.9 billion above the comparison-country trajectory over the first year. That equals roughly 75% of the crisis countries' $10.4 billion pre-crisis holdings. The sample comprises 21 crisis countries during 2021–2025 and 86 matched emerging and developing control economies.

The BIS study published July 21, by Boris Hofmann, Aaron Mehrotra and Jan Paulick, also finds persistence in dollar deposits and stablecoin activity. It reports limited evidence that the two forms of dollar exposure substitute for one another.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Its restrictions findings look different from Reuter's: estimated stablecoin-flow responses are not statistically significant. But the studies do not run the same test.

The BIS working paper uses Chainalysis gross USDT and USDC inflows across 184 countries during 2017–2024. It allocates transfers involving entities such as exchanges using website traffic. Reuter's panel ends a year later and uses a different wallet-location model. The BIS study's broad deposit panel, meanwhile, covers more than 130 economies during 1990–2019, a much longer historical record than either stablecoin dataset.

Their policy comparisons differ too. Reuter studies changes around activations. BIS includes comparisons across restriction regimes and quarterly Latin American panels using weighted restriction indices and stablecoin-specific rules. An association with the rules in place is different from a response around the month a rule changes.

Those differences prevent a simple verdict that one study disproves the other. Nor does a statistically insignificant estimate establish that restrictions can never affect stablecoin use.

Dollarization can mean people holding foreign currency instead of domestic money. Demonstrating that preference is different from measuring whether a central bank's interest-rate decisions become less effective.

The BIS researchers address monetary transmission using deposit dollarization and policy surprises in 14 emerging-market economies during 2000–2024. They find limited evidence of significant differences in transmission by deposit-dollarization level. That test does not estimate the direct causal effect of stablecoin adoption.

They also find moderate deposit dollarization associated with somewhat higher inflation risks.

The authors warn that experience with deposits may not translate adequately to tokens circulating beyond regulated banks and serving different purposes.

CryptoSlate's earlier coverage of crisis-linked stablecoin demand examined the enforcement challenge created by dollar access outside domestic banking channels. The post-removal evidence extends that discussion by asking whether the alternative remains in use after the original restriction ends.

For authorities easing controls, the practical implication is to keep measuring digital-dollar use after the rule changes. A decline in transfers, a decline in dollar holdings and a stronger response to domestic interest rates would each tell a different story.

#5117. BTCPay urges Bitcoin merchants to replace wallets after hackers steal an estimated $1.6 million

The payment software provider warns that installing security updates may not remove attackers' access, as Bitcoin and Litecoin thefts continue.

BTCPay Server has urged merchants to rebuild outdated payment servers and replace wallets after attackers stole Bitcoin and Litecoin.

In a Nov. 2 security advisory, the open-source Bitcoin payment software provider warned that installing the latest update may not protect funds if hackers have already gained access to a server.

The warning follows a series of attacks targeting installations running version 2.4.1 or earlier, using a different method to exploit a vulnerability that BTCPay patched on Aug. 7.

Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery traced approximately $1.63 million in suspected thefts involving 18.38 BTC and 3,405 LTC across 682 wallet sweeps between Oct. 30 and Nov. 2.

Its investigation found that 87% of the funds moved during an eight-hour window on Nov. 1, with the busiest hour accounting for approximately $725,000 in transfers.

The findings provide an early indication of the attack's financial scale, although BTCPay has not confirmed a total loss figure or established how many merchants were compromised.

BTCPay said it regretted not doing more to reach operators still running outdated installations after its August security advisory. The project has issued several additional security updates since then, including version 2.4.7 on Oct. 29.

The suspected thefts continued even after BTCPay publicly warned operators to shut down their servers.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

According to Bitquery, another 267 wallet sweeps worth approximately $150,000 occurred after BTCPay's Nov. 1 warning at 22:25 UTC. The transfers continued through the night and were still being recorded when the analytics firm stopped counting at 06:00 UTC on Nov. 2.

BTCPay said it contacted hosting providers, including LunaNode, to take affected installations offline as a precaution. The project had received its first emailed theft report at 20:56 UTC, although its investigation documented a wallet drain earlier that afternoon.

The company said it had seen no impact from these attacks on servers already running version 2.4.2 or later when the November warning was issued. It also confirmed that wallets whose spending keys were stored outside the server, including hardware wallets, were unaffected by the reported thefts.

However, BTCPay is urging operators who previously ran vulnerable versions to migrate to entirely new server installations, even if they have since updated their software or have not noticed any missing funds.

The concern is that attackers who previously gained administrative access may have obtained credentials or wallet secrets that remain usable after the underlying vulnerability is patched.

Consequently, the project recommends generating a new wallet with a fresh seed phrase on a trusted device and transferring any remaining funds from the old hot wallet.

Operators must also retire their existing servers, preserve transaction records and relevant logs, and install the latest supported software on a fresh virtual machine without restoring the old system.

Potentially exposed hosting passwords, SSH credentials, application access keys, API keys and Lightning credentials should also be replaced from a trusted device.

Meanwhile, Bitquery's tracking showed that more than 99% of the suspected stolen funds remained at addresses first used during the thefts as of early Nov. 2, with no identified transfers to known exchanges.

The analytics firm cautioned that its 682 recorded sweeps do not represent an equivalent number of affected merchants, as individual wallets could have been drained through multiple transactions.

BTCPay has also warned that some activity involving the identified addresses appears to predate the reported incidents. It has yet to establish the complete attack timeline or determine which transactions can be conclusively attributed to the thefts.

The project is continuing to collect reports and transaction records from affected operators as it investigates the intrusions.

#5116. Intel Stock Suffers Worst Crash in 50 Years as Weak Forecast Overshadows AI Chip Demand

Intel Stock Suffers Worst Crash in 50 Years as Weak Forecast Overshadows AI Chip Demand

Intel shares plunged nearly 30% to $65.76 on Friday after the chipmaker issued a fourth-quarter forecast that fell well short of Wall Street expectations, overshadowing an otherwise strong earnings report.

The selloff wiped out more than $146 billion in market value, and translated into the worst single-day decline in more than 50 years.

The problem is that Intel finds itself in an unusual position: demand for its processors is booming because of the AI infrastructure buildout, but the company cannot manufacture enough chips to capitalize on it. At the same time, its traditional PC business is struggling, and the factories needed to support its long-term recovery are consuming enormous amounts of money.

Intel's third-quarter numbers were actually quite good. Revenue climbed 21% year over year to $16.5 billion, while adjusted earnings per share reached $0.52, beating the company's guidance of $0.38 and Wall Street's $0.40 estimate. It was the company's eighth consecutive quarter of exceeding its financial expectations, according to CFO Dave Zinsner.

The strongest performer was Intel's Data Center and AI division, which generated $7.3 billion in revenue, up 78% year over year and nearly 80% compared with the same period last year. The division's operating profit reached $3 billion, representing 42% of its revenue.

But investors were looking at what comes next. Intel expects fourth-quarter revenue between $15 billion and $16 billion, meaning its midpoint guidance of $15.5 billion is roughly $1.6 billion below the $17.07 billion analysts had anticipated. Adjusted earnings per share are expected to fall to $0.25, compared with Wall Street's $0.43 estimate.

Gross margins, which measure how much revenue remains after accounting for the direct costs of making products, are also expected to deteriorate. Intel forecast a non-GAAP gross margin of 38% for Q4, down from 44.1% in the third quarter.

Three problems explain much of that deterioration: weaker PC demand, manufacturing constraints affecting server processors, and an accident at one of Intel's factories in Israel.

Intel CFO Dave Zinsner described 2027 as an investment year and warned investors not to expect positive free cash flow. Free cash flow is the money a company has left after paying operating expenses and investments in equipment and infrastructure. Intel expects to sacrifice cash generation in the short term to finance its manufacturing expansion.

"Probably not a dynamic where we are going to have positive free cash flow next year," Zinsner said during the earnings call. He added that Intel expects a return on invested capital of at least 20% on its manufacturing node investments, but acknowledged that several major projects will require substantial spending at the same time.

That spending is part of Intel's broader attempt to rebuild its manufacturing business and compete with Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, or TSMC, the world's leading contract chipmaker. Intel wants to manufacture chips not only for its own products but also for outside customers, a business model known as a foundry.

The turnaround has attracted major partners. In September 2025, Nvidia invested $5 billion in Intel and agreed to collaborate on custom processors for PCs and data centers. The partnership gave Intel a financial boost while connecting its processors to Nvidia's AI ecosystem.

Intel's next-generation 18A process, the manufacturing technology behind chips such as Panther Lake, is central to the company's attempt to regain its technological edge.

More recently, a preliminary agreement with Apple to manufacture some of its chips helped send Intel shares above $130 in May. The potential deal could give Intel a significant new customer as Apple looks to diversify its manufacturing supply chain.

Those developments helped fuel optimism around Intel's recovery, but Friday's selloff shows how much still needs to go right. The company has strong demand in its data center business, yet it must increase manufacturing output, navigate a shrinking PC market and finance an expensive expansion before investors can expect consistent cash generation.

#5110. Crypto investigators flag suspected $106M theft, $786K Solana bot drain

A European entity may have lost $106 million from exchange accounts, while a wallet associated with a Solana arbitrage bot may have lost another $786,000.

An unidentified entity in the European Union may have lost more than $106 million from accounts at multiple cryptocurrency exchanges on Sunday, according to blockchain investigator ZachXBT.

In a Monday post, ZachXBT said funds suspected to have been stolen were withdrawn in USDC and USDT before being moved through several cryptocurrencies and blockchains.

The suspected attacker swapped USDC for Ether (ETH), used instant exchange services including WellWallet and Kyrrex, and converted funds into Monero (XMR) through the swap service Wagyu.

The suspected attacker remains unidentified, though ZachXBT said the movement of funds pointed away from a link to North Korea’s Lazarus Group. The affected entity and exchanges involved have not been publicly named.

ZachXBT identified 11 wallet addresses associated with the funds.

The reported theft follows the $6.3 billion Binance hack on Oct. 29, which the FBI has attributed to North Korean hackers known as “TraderTraitor,” a group linked to Lazarus. The attack was the largest cryptocurrency theft on record.

Related: Binance net outflows surge to $5.8B as withdrawals resume after record hack

A wallet associated with King, an automated trading bot on Solana, may have lost about $786,000 on Monday, according to pseudonymous blockchain monitoring account Onchain Lens.

King is an arbitrage bot, a software that automatically buys cryptocurrency in one market and sells it in another to profit from price differences. Onchain Lens told Cointelegraph the funds moved through several Solana wallets before being transferred to Ethereum using Mayan, a service for moving assets between blockchains. It said the rapid transfers suggested an attempt to conceal the funds’ origin and a possible wallet compromise.

King’s operator has not confirmed a compromise.

“Our report is based on unusual fund movements, rapid transfers and the subsequent cross-chain movement of funds, which we believe indicates an attempt to obscure their origin,” Onchain Lens told Cointelegraph. “This pattern strongly suggests a potential wallet compromise.”
